===== PAGE 3 =====
What’s Inside
To know more about us, explore 
piramalpharma.com 
At Piramal Pharma Limited (PPL), 
sustainability shapes how we operate, 
how we make decisions and how we stay 
accountable to those who place their 
trust in us. It is reflected in the discipline 
with which we follow through on our 
commitments and in the clarity with 
which we communicate our progress. 
Against this backdrop, we present our 
Sustainability Report for FY2026, covering 
non-financial performance for the period 
from April 1, 2025, to March 31, 2026.
The past year represented an important 
phase in PPL’s sustainability journey. It 
was marked by visible outcomes and also 
by stronger systems, sharper priorities 
and a more embedded approach to 
responsible growth. Across operations, 
governance and community engagement, 
sustainability considerations increasingly 
informed the way we planned, 
invested and executed.
This report brings together our actions 
and outcomes across the Environment, 
Social and Governance pillars. During the 
year, our focus areas included accelerating 
the shift towards energy-efficient 
technologies, increasing the share of 
renewable energy in our operations 
and strengthening circular practices 
through improved waste segregation and 
resource management. We advanced 
water conservation efforts and expanded 
community programmes with a sustained 
emphasis on education and skill 
development. At our sites, health, safety 
and employee well-being stayed central 
to how work is designed and delivered.
We saw renewed attention on governance 
foundations during the year. We 
strengthened internal policies to further 
embed ethical business practices and 
undertook structured risk assessments 
to reinforce transparency, oversight and 
decision-making across the organisation.
Together, these efforts represent our 
steady progress in translating intent 
into action. This report is intended to 
offer stakeholders a clear, coherent 
view of PPL’s sustainability priorities, the 
choices we are making and the impact 
we are creating.
Reporting Frameworks and 
Standards
This Sustainability Report has been 
prepared in accordance with the Global 
Reporting Initiative (GRI) Universal 
Standards 2021. It addresses all nine 
disclosure requirements under GRI 1: 
Foundation, GRI 2: General Disclosures 
and GRI 3: Material Topics.
Our disclosures are also aligned 
with the United Nations Sustainable 
Development Goals (UN SDGs), 
reflecting our commitment to 
responsible business practices that are 
relevant in a global context.
Reporting Period, Scope and 
Boundary2
The information disclosed in this report 
relates to FY2026, covering the period 
from April 1, 2025, to March 31, 2026. 
The reporting scope includes three 
business units: Piramal Pharma Solutions 
(PPS), Piramal Critical Care (PCC) and 
Piramal Consumer Healthcare (PCH).
These businesses operate development 
and manufacturing facilities across India, 
the United Kingdom and North America. 
The scope and boundary of the report 
are consistent with PPL’s consolidated 
annual financial statements. 
The Company is headquartered 
in Mumbai, India.
Materiality
The set of material topics reported 
this year is consistent with previous 
reporting periods, unless stated 
otherwise. These topics were identified 
through structured stakeholder 
engagement and a rigorous materiality 
assessment process.
The report is designed to communicate 
PPL’s key sustainability priorities, long 
term objectives and targets, progress 
achieved during the year and the 
outcomes generated for relevant 
stakeholder groups.
Accountability
PPL’s sustainability disclosures are 
aligned with applicable regulatory 
requirements and stakeholder 
expectations. All disclosures undergo 
internal review and validation by cross 
functional teams, with appropriate 
management oversight to ensure 
accuracy, completeness and consistency.
Contact3
We value transparent and continuous 
engagement with our stakeholders. 
Queries, comments or feedback on 
this report may be directed to our 
sustainability spokesperson at 
ganesh.tripathy@piramal.com 
or through 
ppl.sustainability@piramal.com.
Forward-Looking Statement
This report includes forward-looking 
statements relating to the Company’s 
strategies, initiatives, targets and 
expected performance. Such statements 
are based on current assumptions and 
estimates and are subject to known 
and unknown risks and uncertainties. 
Terms such as “will”, “believes”, 
“estimates”, “aims”, “anticipates” and 
“plans” are intended to identify forward 
looking intent.
Actual results may differ materially. 
The Company does not undertake any 
obligation to update forward-looking 
statements considering future events or 
new information.
External Assurance4
Key sustainability disclosures in this 
report have been independently assured 
to enhance credibility and reliability. The 
external assurance has been carried out 
by DNV Business Assurance India Pvt 
Ltd. The assurance statement is provided 
as an annexure to this report.
Restatements5
There were no material restatements 
during the reporting period.

Leadership Message1
"At Piramal Pharma, we believe 
innovation is most meaningful when 
it creates lasting value. By advancing 
innovation, we are strengthening 
our ability to deliver better 
outcomes for patients, support our 
stakeholders and contribute to a 
more sustainable future."
At Piramal Pharma Limited, we recognise that our responsibility 
extends far beyond the products we manufacture and 
the services we deliver. We are part of a larger ecosystem 
that touches millions of lives through patients, healthcare 
professionals, customers, communities and partners. The 
decisions we make today have implications that extend well into 
the future. This understanding shapes the way we approach 
growth, innovation and sustainability. For us, sustainability is 
embedded in how we create value and fulfil our purpose of 
"Doing Well and Doing Good”.
The pharmaceutical industry is undergoing significant 
transformation. Advances in science and technology are 
unlocking new possibilities in healthcare, while expectations 
around quality, transparency and responsible business 
conduct continue rising. Layered onto these challenges are the 
realities of climate change, resource constraints and evolving 
regulatory requirements. 
Against this backdrop, we stay focused on building a 
business that is equipped for the future. During FY2026, we 
strengthened the foundations that supported sustainable 
growth across our three business verticals. From enhancing 
operational excellence and advancing digital transformation to 
reducing environmental impacts and investing in our people, 
SAP S/4HANA progressed, enhancing enterprise integration 
and enabling data-driven decision-making across functions. 
In parallel, the Laboratory Information Management System 
footprint was also expanded to strengthen data integrity.
Reducing Environmental Impact Through 
Responsible Operations
We stay conscious of the environmental impact associated 
with our manufacturing. We rely on significant consumption of 
energy, water and material resources, making it imperative to 
actively manage our environmental footprint. Guided by our 
Science-Based Targets initiative-validated commitments.
During FY2026, we made encouraging progress across several 
environmental performance indicators. Increased focus on 
sourcing renewable power and Green Fuel resulted in 41% of total 
energy consumption We achieved a 22.6% reduction in emissions 
compared to the FY2022 baseline, marking it the lowest-ever 
Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions for PPL. Alongside our efforts 
to decarbonise operations, we continued improving our water 
stewardship efforts. During the year, we were able to save over 2.63 
Lakh kilolitres of freshwater.
Environmental stewardship extends beyond energy and water 
management. During the year we continued progressing towards 
higher recycling rates for non-hazardous waste across our sites. 
More than 6,520 saplings were planted during the year, helping 
maintain over 30% green cover across our Indian operations, 
contributing to healthier local ecosystems.
Enabling Our People for Exponential Success
The strength of Piramal Pharma has always been rooted in its 
people. Our employees bring expertise, dedication and a shared 
sense of purpose to everything we do. During FY2026, there 
was a 20.1% share of women in the total workforce, a 19% 
share in management positions in revenue-generating functions 
and a 21% share in STEM-related positions. Through initiatives 
such as ScaleUp, Piramal Leadership Series, Piramal Executive 
Council and our Diversity, Inclusion, Belonging and Accessibility 
programmes, our managers participated in efforts designed 
to foster a culture where every individual feels respected and 
included. Learning and development stayed a priority, with 
500+ employees participating in various programmes delivered 
through the Functional Academies, supporting both individual 
growth and organisational capability building.
Equally important is our commitment to safeguarding the Well-
being of our workforce. Safety is fundamental to our operations 
because behind every performance metric is a person, a family and 
a community. Through targeted interventions and transformation 
initiatives, such as implementation of safety frameworks, namely 
BICFT, IICFT, CCFT and SCFT, along with promotion of a Behaviour-
Based Safety (BBS) culture, we have strengthened safe practices 
and employee engagement across our operations. Our aspiration is 
to build a culture where safety is instinctive, deeply embedded and 
embraced by everyone across the organisation.
Extending Responsibility across  
Our Value Chain
Our responsibility does not end at the boundaries of our 
operations. We believe that meaningful progress requires 
collaboration across the value chain. Through our Sustainable 
Procurement Policy and our participation in the Pharmaceutical 
Supply Chain Initiative, we continued engaging suppliers on 
environmental, social and governance priorities. This year 38% 
of our critical suppliers were assessed against ESG criteria and 
supported through capability-building initiatives designed to 
build responsible business practices throughout the supply chain.
Creating Meaningful Social Impact
Beyond business operations, we stay committed to creating 
positive social impact through the work at Piramal Foundation. 
During the year, investments of ₹8.98 Crore supported health 
and education programmes across 112 aspirational districts, 
positively impacting approximately ~1.8 Million people. These 
initiatives reflect our belief that business success should 
contribute to broader societal progress and help create 
opportunities for underserved communities.
Strategic Partnerships and Expansions
Building forward-looking capabilities is a key priority as we 
position Piramal Pharma to address emerging therapeutic needs 
and support the next generation of healthcare innovation. 
Through partnerships with IntoCell, Blue-Zone Technologies, 
Aji Bio-Pharma and NewAmsterdam Pharma, we expanded 
innovation capabilities, enhanced manufacturing excellence and 
advanced sustainability-focused initiatives across our business.
We strengthened our advanced therapeutics platform through 
a US$90 Million investment across our Lexington and Riverview 
facilities. We are also expanding our formulation, sterile 
injectable, payload-linker and bioconjugation capabilities to 
support next-generation drug development and manufacturing.
As we look ahead, we are encouraged by the opportunities 
emerging across the healthcare sector. At Piramal Pharma, 
we will continue investing in the capabilities, technologies 
and partnerships that support our business and enable 
responsible growth. 
We are grateful for the trust placed in us by our employees, 
customers, investors, partners and communities. Their support 
continues inspiring us as we work towards creating enduring 
value and contributing to a healthier, more sustainable future.
With warm regards,
Nandini Piramal

As we present our fifth Sustainability Report, we reflect on a 
year defined by progress, learning and purposeful action. This 
year's theme, "Advancing Innovation, Leveraging Sustainability”, 
captures our belief that innovation and sustainability are not 
parallel priorities but powerful enablers of one another. We 
are focused on ensuring that innovation is pursued responsibly 
and supported by sustainable practices that create value for 
our stakeholders. 
Building Trust through  
Quality and Excellence
A defining focus area during the year was strengthening the 
systems and capabilities that will support our future ambitions. 
Customers, regulators and patients place immense confidence 
in the quality and reliability of the products we manufacture. 
During the year, customer satisfaction scores reached 85% in 
our CDMO business and 87% in our hospital generics business. 
Supported by our quality management systems, we successfully 
completed 38 regulatory inspections as well as 209 customer 
audits. We also laid the groundwork for initiating a unified 
digital quality management system to enhance standardisation 
and end‑to‑end visibility along with establishing an integrated 
quality cockpit combining our programmes ‘Sensor’, ‘Predict’ 
and ‘Calculus’ for real‑time and predictive insights.

a critical enabler of efficiency, agility and decision-making.

===== PAGE 7 =====
Piramal Pharma in Focus
Piramal Pharma Limited was founded in March 2020 with a clear global 
orientation. Since its inception, the Company has built a commercial presence 
across more than 100 countries, supported by a network of 17 development 
and manufacturing facilities. Together, these capabilities enable end-to-end 
solutions across a broad pharmaceutical spectrum and form the operational 
backbone of PPL’s offerings.
The origins of the Company, however, extend further back. 
PPL traces its lineage to Piramal Enterprises Limited (PEL), a 
long-standing participant in the pharmaceutical sector. As 
part of a strategic realignment, the pharmaceutical business 
was consolidated under Piramal Pharma Limited in October 
2021. On October 19, 2022, Piramal Pharma Limited was 
listed on both the BSE and NSE, establishing its position as 
a standalone entity with a distinct strategic mandate and 
governance structure. 
Who We Are1 
Today, Piramal Pharma Limited offers a portfolio of 
differentiated products and services delivered through its 
17 global development and manufacturing facilities and an 
extensive distribution network spanning over 100 countries. 
The Company operates through three distinct businesses, 
each addressing specific segments of the pharmaceutical 
value chain while collectively shaping PPL’s integrated model.
  12-6
Doing Well and Doing Good
PPL’s purpose is anchored in advancing healthcare outcomes that create meaningful impact. This commitment shapes the 
development of specialised, high-quality therapies and differentiated products designed to address critical and unmet 
patient needs. The emphasis on purpose-led growth reflects the belief that sustainable value creation and positive societal 
impact are inherently linked.
Expertise
 Innovation

a drive to innovate.
Trusteeship

guide to ensure wellness for all.

 Resilience
Amidst a dynamic landscape of healthcare, we believe 
impact is a culmination of both performance that delivers 
value to our people and the resilience to adapt and lead.
Our Business Blueprint for Purposeful Impact1
The complexity of modern diseases demands approaches that 
extend beyond traditional models. As healthcare challenges 
become more nuanced, responding to unmet patient needs 
requires agility, along with a foundation of care and trust. PPL’s 
business blueprint reflects this reality, delivering healthcare 
and well being solutions across the pharmaceutical value chain.
Piramal Pharma Solutions serves a customer base of over 500 
organisations, including large pharmaceutical companies, 
generic manufacturers and emerging biotech firms. Its 
capabilities span discovery, development and large-scale 
manufacturing, supported by a global footprint across North 
America, the UK and Europe, and India. This network enables 
flexibility, speed and consistent quality across the drug 
development lifecycle.
With operations in more than 100 countries, Piramal Critical 
Care is a global provider of hospital generics, with strength in 
inhaled anaesthetics and specialty injectable products. 
Its portfolio includes intrathecal therapies and pain 
management solutions, reaching over 6,000 hospitals, surgical 
centres and veterinary clinics worldwide. This scale positions 
PCC as a trusted partner in acute and critical care delivery.
Addressing everyday health needs, the Piramal Consumer 
Healthcare business offers trusted OTC solutions spanning pain 
management, skin care, nutritional supplements, analgesics, 
children’s wellness and women’s health and hygiene. Its 
portfolio of over 30 OTC products is further strengthened 
through a strategic agreement with Bayer Pharmaceuticals 
Private Limited for the distribution of established brands such 
as Saridon, Supradyn, Becozym and Benadon.
Together, these businesses, Piramal Pharma Solutions, Piramal 
Critical Care and Piramal Consumer Healthcare, define Piramal 
Pharma Limited’s integrated operating model, aligning purpose, 
performance and impact across the healthcare landscape.
Our Belief 
System:  
Our Tenets
Making a positive difference to the health of our patients and customers through our 
products and services.
Serving our patients, customers, consumers, community, employees, partners, stakeholders, 
and our planet.
Living our values in our everyday actions, decisions, and conduct at a personal and corporate level.
•	Piramal Pharma Solutions (PPS) functions as an integrated 
Contract Development and Manufacturing Organisation, 
supporting pharmaceutical innovation from early 
development through commercial manufacturing. 
•	Piramal Critical Care (PCC) focuses on the Complex Hospital 
Generics space, serving critical care requirements across 
global markets. 
•	Piramal Consumer Healthcare (PCH) complements these 
businesses by offering over the counter healthcare and 
wellness products to consumers.
Beyond its core operations, PPL’s ecosystem includes strategic 
partnerships and investments. AbbVie Therapeutics India 
Private Limited, an associate company, operates as a joint 
venture with AbbVie Inc. and has emerged as a market 
leader in the ophthalmology therapy area. PPL also maintains 
a minority investment in Yapan Bio Private Limited. In 
October 2020, the Company received a 20% strategic growth 
investment from the Carlyle Group, advancing its long-term 
expansion plans. This structure exists in service of a single, 
unifying purpose that guides everything we do.

Business Performance
FY2026 represented a year of measured and resilient performance for 
Piramal Pharma Limited, reflecting steady execution in a challenging 
global environment. Supported by balanced contributions across all three 
business segments-Piramal Pharma Solutions, Piramal Critical Care, and 
Piramal Consumer Healthcare, our total revenue reached ₹8,869 Crores, 
demonstrating the strength of the Company’s diversified operating model.
The CDMO business played a central role in overall performance, accounting for 55% of total revenue with ₹4,915 Crores; our 
Hospital Generics business brought in ₹2,703 Crores, contributing to 30% of total revenue; and our consumer health portfolio 
contributed to 14% of the total revenue with ₹1,274 Crores.
During the year, PPL effectively navigated external headwinds 
while continuing to build its global platform. This expanding 
footprint supported customer engagement across markets and 
contributed to long-term growth continuity.
FY2026 was a transition year, shaped by inventory destocking, 
slower early-stage order inflows during the first half and slower 
demand for inhalation anaesthesia in ex-US markets. Excluding 
the impact of inventory destocking, the business recorded 
growth during the year. Cost optimisation and operational 
excellence initiatives helped mitigate the impact on EBITDA, 
while continued investments in capacity, capabilities and 
new products have positioned the business for a return to 
growth in FY2027. Financial performance was underpinned 
by equally disciplined attention to the non-financial risks that 
could affect it.
Sustainability and risk oversight were integral to business 
operations throughout the year. Climate-related risks and 
opportunities were actively monitored, with no material 
impact on operations, revenues, or expenditures reported. 
This experience highlighted the robustness of PPL’s business 
model and its ability to perform consistently amid an 
increasingly complex and dynamic global landscape.

Business Strategy
Responsible growth calls for clear ambition, but achieving it depends on a 
practical path that respects both people and the environment at every stage. 
PPL approaches this responsibility with intent, ensuring that progress is guided 
by principles rather than power alone.
A well-defined sustainability framework shapes how the organisation 
operates across the business. Ethical conduct, environmental stewardship 
and social responsibility are integrated into everyday decisions, enabling PPL 
to manage sustainability risks thoughtfully while reducing potential impacts. 
This disciplined approach supports long-term value creation and reinforces 
accountability across operations. At its core, the sustainability framework is 
built on four pillars:
Business Resilience, Quality and Excellence,  
Responsible Operations, and Stakeholder Centricity.
Sustainability Purpose
Operating Responsibly. 
Growing Sustainably.
Sustainability Vision
To become a globally leading integrated 
pharmaceutical company, operating on the 
principles of sustainability, inclusivity, and ethics.
Our Sustainability Strategy 
PPL’s sustainability strategy is built on a clear and structured 
foundation. At its core are four strategic pillars that provide 
direction and stability, together supporting 12 defined 
sustainability focus areas across the organisation.
These focus areas address 17 material topics identified through 
detailed engagement and deliberation with stakeholders. To 
ensure clarity and accountability, each material topic is further 
translated into more than 50 specific, time-bound targets. 
This granular structure allows progress to be tracked in a 
measurable and systematic manner.
Oversight of the sustainability agenda rests with the 
Sustainability and Risk Management Committee, chaired by 
the Chairperson. The Committee is responsible for monitoring 
performance against established targets and ensuring that 
initiatives stay aligned with the Company’s broader risk and 
governance framework.
Through this disciplined approach, PPL strengthens its ability 
to honour sustainability commitments and deliver tangible 
outcomes across operations, reinforcing responsibility as an 
integral part of long-term business performance.
Sustainability scales when it becomes the smarter way of doing business. At Piramal Consumer 
Healthcare, our focus is to embed responsible choices into how we design, source, manufacture, 
package, and distribute—so that sustainability strengthens both consumer value and environmental 
impact. Sustainability creates the greatest impact when it is built into the business model itself—
making products better for consumers, operations more efficient, and growth more responsible."
Sai Ponugoti 
CEO – Piramal Consumer Healthcare

Stakeholder Engagement1
Stakeholder engagement is a central element of how PPL 
sustains operational effectiveness and creates long-term value. 
The Company prioritises active engagement and collaboration 
with stakeholders across the value chain, recognising their 
role in shaping resilient and responsible business outcomes. 
Through structured materiality assessments, stakeholders 
directly contribute to identifying sustainability priorities and 
informing organisational goals.
Key internal and external stakeholder groups are identified 
based on their potential influence on, or exposure to, business 
activities. This deliberate mapping ensures that sustainability 
efforts stay inclusive, relevant and forward looking. By 
anchoring decisions in stakeholder perspectives, PPL works to 
reinforce transparency, build trust, and align business practices 
with evolving expectations.
Ongoing engagement is maintained through a range of 
mechanisms calibrated to the level of mutual influence. 
Regular interactions take place through meetings, surveys, 
digital platforms and forums, enabling continuous dialogue and 
timely responses to stakeholder concerns. These exchanges 
support informed decision-making, help refine sustainability 
priorities and build accountability across the value chain.
Through this structured and inclusive approach, PPL fosters 
durable relationships with stakeholders, supporting sustained 
collaboration and shared value creation over the long-term.

  Business Resilience
Material topics such as ethical marketing, data privacy 
and security, supply chain integrity and risk management 
support the Business Resilience pillar by enhancing 
governance, regulatory compliance, cybersecurity and supply 
chain continuity. 
  Quality and Excellence
This pillar is driven by innovation and R&D together with 
sustainable labelling and packaging, reflecting Piramal 
Pharma's commitment to product quality, responsible 
innovation and lifecycle stewardship. 
  Responsible Operations
Climate-related topics including greenhouse gas emissions, 
energy, water, waste and biodiversity underpin the 
Responsible Operations pillar, guiding actions to strengthen 
resource stewardship, environmental performance and 
operational resilience. 
  Stakeholder Centricity
Material topics related to patient safety and quality, access 
and affordability, occupational health and safety, employee 
development, human rights and talent retention shape the 
Stakeholder Centricity pillar, reinforcing the Company's focus 
on patient outcomes, workforce well-being, ethical business 
practices and lasting stakeholder relationships. 
Double Materiality Assessment1
During FY2026, our materiality assessment was refreshed using a Double 
Materiality approach based on the principles of EFRAG and the Global 
Reporting Initiative (GRI) Standards. As part of this process, we covered a wide 
spectrum of impact areas, including the environment, people, human rights, 
supply chain, governance, community and the economy. 
To ensure breadth and depth of insight, a wide range of stakeholder groups were actively engaged. This inclusive engagement 
approach ensured that material topics reflected both internal priorities and external expectations. In line with our materiality 
assessment cycle, this assessment is undertaken once every three years to ensure continued alignment with the evolving business 
context, stakeholder expectations and sustainability priorities.
Step 2
Step 1
We established a comprehensive understanding of the value chain and principal business activities, assessed the Company's 
ESG performance, priorities and emerging sustainability trends. 
	
↗Collaborated with business representatives to evaluate 
sustainability-related impacts, risks and opportunities (IROs).
	
↗Refined the identified impacts and risks by aligning 
them with our enterprise and site-level risk registers, 
eliminating overlaps and consolidating the assessment 
across environmental, social and governance themes for 
subsequent departmental discussions.
The next phase focused on identifying and prioritising 
key stakeholder groups and designing a comprehensive 
stakeholder engagement process. 
	
↗We mapped and validated impacts with business 
functions and leadership to finalise the assessment scope, 
considering impacts on people, the environment and 
financial performance.
	
↗The assessment covered all entities under the Company’s 
control or influence, including Piramal Pharma Solutions, 
Piramal Consumer Health, and Piramal Critical Care. 
Severity Assessment
Stakeholders evaluated identified impacts based on 
scale, scope and likelihood, assigning High, Medium 
or Low ratings based on the magnitude, extent and 
probability of occurrence to refine the impact universe 
for financial assessment.
Financial Assessment
Shortlisted impacts were assessed for their potential 
influence on financial performance and long-term 
value creation and prioritised using predefined financial 
materiality thresholds. These thresholds enabled a 
consistent and objective evaluation of financially material 
sustainability topics.
Our Approach 
The exercise began with clearly defining the boundaries. PPL developed a holistic understanding of its organisational 
context, encompassing business activities, relationships, stakeholder interactions, and the broader sustainability landscape.
Step 3
Our assessment identified 17 material topics that shape our sustainability strategy, risk management approach, and ESG 
disclosures. The topics remain largely consistent with the previous assessment, with select refinements to enhance relevance 
and clarity. The assessment was approved by the Board, independently assured, and embedded within the Company's 
Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) framework.
Our Materiality Matrix
The outcomes of the materiality assessment played a critical role in aligning the Company's business strategy, long-term 
objectives and planning horizons. The topics were then prioritised according to their relative importance and have been 
presented in the form of a materiality matrix. 
A Strategic Link Between Materiality and Sustainability
Our Double Materiality Assessment establishes a direct linkage between our material topics and our Sustainability Framework 
by aligning the Company's most significant impacts, risks and opportunities with its strategic priorities. The foundation of our 
Sustainability Framework is formed by four strategic pillars, forming a strong base for 12 key sustainability focus areas.

Board Leadership and Strategic Oversight2
Guiding Long‑term Value Creation
PPL’s Board of Directors provides strong strategic leadership, 
anchored in integrity, accountability and long‑term value 
creation. The Board brings together accomplished leaders with 
deep expertise across general management, pharmaceuticals, 
public health, science and innovation, marketing and 
commercial, finance, economics, taxation, legal, technology, 
risk management, governance, human resources, sustainability 
& ESG, entrepreneurship, business leadership, manufacturing, 
quality, operations, supply chain and business development. 
This breadth of perspectives strengthens the Board’s ability 
to navigate complexity, anticipate emerging risks and guide 
the Company toward sustainable growth. The Board operates 
within a well-defined one-tier governance framework, ensuring 
clarity of roles, effective decision-making and strong oversight. 
Its functioning is fully aligned with the SEBI (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (‘SEBI Listing 
Regulations’), the Companies Act, 2013 and other applicable 
statutory requirements. The Board is chaired by Ms Nandini 
Piramal, who serves as the Executive Director and Chairperson, 
providing strategic direction and leadership oversight to 
the Company’s governance framework. The Company has 
received independence declarations from all Independent 
Directors and maintains high standards of transparency and 
regulatory compliance.

Board Committees1
To ensure effective governance, sound oversight and focused 
decision-making, PPL has constituted multiple board-level 
committees, each mandated to oversee specific functional 
areas. These committees operate within clearly defined 
terms of reference and are aligned with the applicable 
regulatory framework.
Each committee is empowered to make informed decisions 
or provide recommendations within its delegated authority, 
consistent with board‑approved policies. The deliberations and 
outcomes of committee meetings are regularly reported to the 
Board, enabling comprehensive review, informed discussion 
and timely approval. This structured committee framework 
supports transparency, accountability and sound governance 
across the organisation.

Board Composition and Experience1
The Board is structured to bring together a strong balance 
of independence, experience and sector-relevant expertise. 
As on March 31, 2026, PPL’s Board comprised 10 Directors, 
in full conformity with Regulation 17(1) of the SEBI Listing 
Regulations, Section 149 of the Companies Act, 2013 and 
other applicable regulatory requirements Independent 
Directors constituted 50% of the Board, reinforcing objective 
oversight, balanced governance and effective decision-making. 
The Company has a one-tier governance system.
The Board includes five Independent Directors, namely 
Mr. Jairaj Purandare, Mr. Sridhar Gorthi, Mr. Peter Stevenson, 
Ms. Vibha Paul Rishi and Mr. Amit Soni. The five Non-
Independent Directors comprise Executive Directors 
Ms. Nandini Piramal, Chairperson, Mr. Peter DeYoung 
and Mr. Vivek Valsaraj, along with Non-Executive, Non-
Independent Directors Ms. Nathalie Leitch and Mr. Amit Jain. 
This breadth of expertise strengthens strategic oversight and 
supports the Company’s ambition to be a globally leading 
integrated pharmaceutical company operating on the 
principles of sustainability, inclusivity and ethics.
The Company also follows disciplined norms on external 
commitments. Seven Non-Executive and Independent 
Directors hold three or fewer external board mandates of 
listed companies, supporting focused engagement, informed 
participation and effective contribution to Board deliberations.
More details of our Board of Directors are available in the 
Company’s Annual Report 2025-26 on pages 16-17.
Board Tenure
The Company follows a defined Board tenure framework that 
balances continuity, independence and periodic renewal. The 
average tenure of Board members is 3.52 years. Executive 
Directors are appointed for a term of three years and are 
eligible for reappointment, enabling focused leadership with 
periodic review. Non-Executive Independent Directors are 
appointed for a term of five years and may be re-appointed, 
subject to a maximum of two consecutive terms, in line with 
applicable regulatory requirements. Non-Executive Directors 
do not have a fixed tenure and are governed by retirement and 
re-appointment provisions under the Companies Act, 2013.
This structured approach supports Board stability while 
enabling timely refreshment of skills, experience and 
perspectives. It also reinforces governance discipline, 
strengthens succession planning and ensures continued 
alignment with evolving regulatory expectations, business 
priorities and stakeholder interests.
Executive Directors: 3 Years
Independent Directors: 5 Years
Board Engagement and Effectiveness
During FY2026, the Board met six (06) times, with meetings 
scheduled in advance to support disciplined deliberation. 
Detailed agenda papers were shared at least seven days prior 
to each meeting, except in case of urgent business to be 
transacted at shorter notice enabling informed discussions and 
effective decision-making.
The average Board meeting attendance stood at 95%, reflecting 
strong commitment and active participation by Directors.
Board Nomination, Evaluation and 
Remuneration1
The Company has instituted a comprehensive framework 
governing Board composition, evaluation and remuneration, 
aligned with regulatory requirements and governance 
practices. The Board-approved Nomination Policy sets out 
clear criteria for the selection and appointment of Directors 
and Senior Management, including qualifications, experience, 
independence and other attributes in line with Section 178(3) of 
the Companies Act, 2013.
Aligned with the terms of applicable provisions of the 
Companies Act, 2013, and SEBI Listing Regulations, the Board 
carried out an annual performance evaluation of its own 
performance, that of directors and also Committees of the 
Board. Board Evaluation was conducted through a structured 
questionnaire designed based on the criteria for evaluation and 
the process set by the NRC. Board performance is evaluated 
taking into consideration various performance related aspects. 
The performance of the Executive Directors is evaluated by 
the NRC on the basis of achievement of their key responsibility 
areas and other factors in line with the Remuneration Policy of 
the Company. A report summarising the evaluations, is placed 
before the Board by the Chairman of the NRC, which contains 
the collective impression of the Directors on the functioning of 
the Board, its Committees and Individual Directors. 
During the year, all Directors participated in dedicated sessions 
focused on sustainability, compliance, risk management, 
macroeconomic developments, and business performance.
Independent Directors were further supported through 
dedicated Familiarisation Programmes to enhance their 
understanding of the Company’s strategy, industry dynamics 
and evolving regulatory landscape.
Remuneration and Compensation2
The remuneration framework is designed to be fair, transparent 
and aligned with performance. Executive remuneration 
comprises fixed components and variable pay linked to strategic 
initiatives, individual roles, responsibilities and overall Company 
performance. Non-Executive Directors are compensated 
through sitting fees for participation in Board and Committee 
meetings, along with commission linked to net profits, subject 
to shareholder approval and statutory limits.
The Company also ensures pay equity and transparency through 
periodic disclosures. In FY2026, the ratio of remuneration of 
the highest-paid individual to the median employee stood at 
126:1. Compensation for top earners increased by 74%, while 
the median employee remuneration increased by 0.33% during 
the year. This balanced approach reinforces accountability, 
incentivises performance and supports the Company’s  
long-term value creation objectives.

Sustainability Governance and Oversight2
The Company has established a Board‑level Sustainability and 
Risk Management Committee, chaired by the Chairperson, 
to steer its sustainability agenda and provide oversight of 
ESG performance. The Committee offers strategic guidance 
to ensure alignment with the UN Sustainable Development 
Goals (SDGs), global sustainability frameworks and evolving 
stakeholder expectations.
It plays a pivotal role in integrating ESG considerations 
into the Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) framework by 
identifying and monitoring sustainability‑related risks and 
opportunities and tracking progress against defined targets. 
The Committee also reviews performance across external ESG 
ratings and indices, supporting continuous improvement and 
benchmarking against global best practices.
Through biannual reviews, the Committee approves 
sustainability strategies for material topics, oversees ESG 
disclosures and facilitates structured stakeholder engagement 
on key issues. The Company’s ESG Policy underpins this 
governance framework, reinforcing accountability, resilience 
and a proactive response to emerging sustainability trends.

Management-level Governance
Embedding Governance through a Strong Policy Framework1
At PPL, policies extend beyond regulatory compliance to serve 
as strategic enablers that guide responsible decision-making, 
reinforce accountability and embed sustainability across the 
organisation. Grounded in ethical governance and a commitment 
to long-term value creation, the Company’s policy framework 
provides a clear and consistent foundation for informed actions 
across all levels of the business.
All statutory policies pertaining to Companies Act, 2013 and SEBI 
Listing Regulations are reviewed and approved by the Board or 
its delegated committees, ensuring sound oversight, collective 
stewardship and alignment with regulatory requirements. 
These policies apply across the organisation and are periodically 
reviewed to reflect evolving global standards, emerging risks and 
stakeholder expectations. Together, they enable management to 
take informed, ethical and forward looking decisions that support 
sustainable value creation.
A cohesive policy ecosystem underpinning ESG excellence includes the following:
Anti-Corruption and

Business Ethics and Compliance1
At PPL, compliance is embedded in the way we conduct business and engage 
with stakeholders. It is a shared responsibility that guides our actions, decisions 
and interactions across the Company. 
We have instituted a strong compliance framework to support adherence to applicable laws, regulations, regulatory requirements, 
internal policies and established standards across our operations, facilities, partnerships and value chain. Strengthened by clear 
governance, defined accountability and continuous oversight, this framework provides a strong foundation for responsible 
decision-making and consistent ethical conduct. A key element of this framework is our Code of Conduct, which sets out the 
standards expected from our Directors, senior management and employees. It guides professional conduct, supports full 
regulatory compliance, helps prevent conflicts of interest, protects confidential information and ensures that decisions are taken 
in a fair, transparent and responsible manner.
Code of Conduct and Ethical Guidelines
PPL’s Code of Conduct and Ethical Guidelines define the 
principles that shape our everyday conduct. They cover critical 
areas including conflict of interest, anti-bribery and anti-
corruption, asset protection, animal welfare, product quality, 
responsible sourcing of minerals and fair competition.
Employees are encouraged to report unethical behaviour, 
misconduct or compliance concerns through the anonymous 
‘SpeakUp’ hotline. This provides a secure channel to raise 
concerns without fear of retaliation and supports a culture of 
openness, accountability and trust.
Our governance approach is supported by a strong Zero 
Tolerance Policy towards corruption, bribery and related 
practices.  Anti-corruption measures are embedded within PPL’s 
governance and internal control systems. During the year, risk 
assessments were conducted across 100% of our operations. No 
significant corruption-related risks were identified.
Led by the Board and carried forward by our employees, 
partners and stakeholders, we stay committed to 
strengthening a culture anchored in integrity, transparency and 
responsible conduct.
Zero Tolerance. Zero Compromise.
PPL maintains a strict zero tolerance approach 
towards corruption, bribery, insider trading and 
money laundering.

↗No Discrimination: Commitment to an inclusive

↗Information Confidentiality: Protection of sensitive

channels provided to report violations 
without fear of retaliation.
	
↗Political and Regulatory Integrity: PPL maintains

political and governmental bodies. All interactions are 
disclosed in line with legal requirements.
Key Highlights of our Policy
These commitments were upheld in practice throughout the year, as reflected in our compliance record. During the 
reporting period, no legal actions were pending or completed against the company related to anti-competitive behaviour, 
anti-trust, or monopoly practices. The Company remains committed to fair competition and adheres to all applicable 
competition laws and regulations.
In FY2026, 1 instance of non-compliance led to penalty. Otherwise, there were no major disciplinary actions involving 
Directors, key managerial personnel, or employees.

Whistleblower Protection and Reporting1
PPL’s Whistleblower Policy enables employees and 
stakeholders to raise concerns through a confidential and 
secure ‘SpeakUp’ portal for reporting fraud, misconduct or 
ethical violations. The Company has established a clearly 
defined whistleblowing mechanism, supported by dedicated 
personnel and departments, to ensure that every concern is 
handled responsibly and with appropriate oversight.
The Policy enables the reporting of all known or suspected 
misconduct, malpractices and all unethical, illegal or improper 
activities that have taken place or are suspected to have taken 
place. Its coverage includes abuse of authority; negligence 
causing substantial and specific danger to public health and 
safety; financial irregularities, including fraud or suspected 
fraud; criminal offences; pilferation of confidential/proprietary 
information; misappropriation of company funds/property; 
breach of Code of Conduct & Ethics Policy; harassment of 
any kind; any instances of leak of UPSI; violation of laws 
and regulations of any jurisdiction in which the Company or 
its subsidiaries operate; and any other unethical, immoral 
or illegal events. By clearly defining this scope, the Policy 
strengthens transparency, accountability and ethical conduct 
across PPL and its subsidiaries.
To maintain impartiality, the reporting channels are 
managed by an independent third party. Whistleblowers 
can report concerns anonymously, with strict confidentiality 
maintained at every stage of the process. Retaliation against 
whistleblowers is not tolerated under any circumstances.
Employees receive regular training to understand the use of 
the reporting channel and the process for raising concerns. All 
reported breaches are reviewed through a transparent and 
structured investigation process. The outcomes are placed 
before senior management and the Chairperson of the Audit 
Committee, reinforcing transparency, accountability and trust 
in the system. This same standard of accountability extends to 
how we prevent bribery and corruption across the Company.
Anti-bribery and Anti-corruption Measures1
PPL is firmly committed to conducting business with integrity 
and transparency. The Company has established strong 
anti-bribery and anti-corruption measures, reinforced by 
a Zero Tolerance Policy towards bribery, corruption and 
related practices.
These measures support the prevention, detection and 
mitigation of potential corruption risks while ensuring 
compliance with applicable anti-corruption laws. Our internal 
control systems enable ongoing monitoring of ethical 
practices across operations and business relationships, helping 
strengthen accountability and responsible conduct across 
the organisation.
Key Highlights of our Policy
	
↗The policy is endorsed at the highest level by the Board of 
Directors or Executive Management.
	
↗We endorse zero tolerance for bribery and corruption across 
all business operations and have defined procedures for 
reporting and investigating policy breaches. Additionally, 
disciplinary actions are enforced for any violations.
	
↗There is clear guidance on the acceptance of gifts, 
charitable donations, contributions or sponsorships and 
political contributions. 
	
↗We conduct mandatory training for employees to recognise 
and prevent corrupt practices
PPL conducts mandatory training for all employees and 
management on business ethics, covering topics like anti-
corruption, conflict of interest, competitive practices and the 
Code of Conduct. In FY2026, 100% of employees completed 
training, strengthening the organisation’s commitment to ethics 
and accountability.

Enterprise Risk Management
PPL follows a well-defined Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) framework 
to identify, assess and mitigate risks that may affect operational 
performance, reputation or long-term sustainability. Risks are assessed 
across locations and functions through a structured process, then 
analysed based on their likelihood and potential impact. They are 
subsequently prioritised to ensure timely action. Targeted mitigation 
strategies are implemented to minimise exposure, enhance organisational 
resilience and support uninterrupted operations. Integrating ERM into 
core decision-making strengthens accountability across the business 
and enables us to respond effectively to emerging uncertainties while 
safeguarding stakeholder value.
  Risk Identification
We continuously review both internal operations and the 
external business environment to recognise potential 
events or conditions that may affect the achievement of 
our objectives. This includes engagement with multiple 
stakeholders through workshops and leadership interactions, 
analysis of loss events, periodic review of strategic documents 
and business reports, ongoing tracking of industry trends and 
assessment of regulatory and market developments.
Risk registers are maintained at the site level, material 
subsidiary level and enterprise level. These are refreshed 
annually and updated as required. Key observations and 
outcomes are presented to the Risk Management Committee 
on a half-yearly basis. This structured approach ensures risks 
are identified early and comprehensively, forming a strong 
foundation for effective assessment and mitigation.
   Risk Assessment / Rating
Based on the combined assessment of likelihood and impact, 
risks are systematically prioritised and classified as High, 
Medium or Low. Key risks are then ranked for treatment, and 
appropriate mitigation strategies are developed to address 
them. This systematic approach ensures resources are 
directed towards the most material exposures, strengthening 
organisational resilience and supporting business continuity.

 Risk Treatment / Mitigation
Multiple treatment options are considered based on the nature 
of each risk. Our approach is designed to balance risk exposure 
with business opportunity. Depending on the severity and 
potential impact, the response may include:
Category
Risk Response
Risk Avoidance
Exiting or modifying activities that create 
unacceptable exposure
Risk Acceptance
Accepting the risk where it falls within the defined 
risk appetite
Risk Reduction
Implementing controls and mitigation measures 
to reduce likelihood or impact
Risk Transfer
Using insurance, outsourcing or hedging 
mechanisms
Mitigation actions for critical risks are monitored periodically 
to assess effectiveness and enable timely course correction. 
  Risk Review / Closure
Risk profiles and registers are reviewed periodically to validate 
ratings and evaluate the progress of mitigation plans. Risks 
that have been adequately addressed or are no longer relevant 
due to shifts in the external environment are deprioritised 
following approval from the respective business heads and the 
Chief Risk Officer (CRO).
This ongoing process of review and closure reinforces discipline 
and ensures the risk register stays current and relevant.
Risk Governance Structure
Three-tier Risk Governance Structure
Our risk governance framework follows a three-tier structure, 
ensuring effective oversight, clear accountability and 
independent evaluation:
1 Board of Directors 
The Board of Directors is responsible for reviewing and 
approving the risk management process and policy as well as 
for providing guidance to executive management.
2  Sustainability and Risk Management Committee (SRMC) 
The SRMC, a board-level committee, provides focused 
oversight of enterprise-level risks. It reviews risk reports, 
monitors mitigation progress and evaluates emerging risk 
themes. The Committee meets on a half-yearly basis to assess 
the effectiveness of the ERM framework.
3  Chief Risk Officer (CRO) 
The CRO acts as the central coordinating authority for risk 
management across the Organisation. The CRO consolidates 
risk information from business units, reviews controls as 
well as mitigation plans and presents the consolidated risk 
report to the SRMC.

Identified risks are assessed using two key criteria:
 Most critical. Need active monitoring.
 High impact/ likelihood. Need periodic monitoring.
 Low likelihood & Impact. Need annual review.

Major Risks and Mitigating Actions
The major risks perceived by the Company, along with the measures taken to mitigate them, are as follows:
Probable Risks      
Risk Description
Mitigation Measures
Macroeconomic  
Environment
E
Macroeconomic uncertainties, including trade 
tariffs, geopolitical developments, supply chain 
disruptions, inflationary pressures and their 
impact on interest rate cycles, as well as volatility 
in cross-currency exchange rates, pose potential 
risks to our operations, business model, financial 
performance and customer preferences.
We operate a global network of development 
and manufacturing facilities designed to 
meet the evolving geographic preferences 
and requirements of our customers. To 
minimise supply chain disruptions, such as 
extended transit times from longer shipping 
routes or container shortages, we proactively 
adjust inventory levels, production schedules 
and sourcing plans. Additionally, we maintain 
a Board-approved Currency Hedging Policy 
and a dedicated Forex Risk Management 
Committee, which periodically reviews and 
manages cross-currency exposures and 
hedging positions.

Environment
E
An incomplete and inconsistent recovery in 
biotech funding could reduce CDMO orders, 
particularly for early-stage discovery and 
development projects, and may also lead to 
order deferments due to delays in customer 
decision-making.
We serve a diversified customer base 
comprising large pharmaceutical companies, 
emerging biotech firms and generic drug 
manufacturers. To strengthen engagement, 
we have expanded our Business Development 
team and tailored our marketing strategies 
to evolving market dynamics, enabling more 
effective outreach to both large pharma and 
mid-size biotech customers. Across our global 
network, we stay committed to delivering 
a consistent, reliable experience that drives 
customer satisfaction and delight.

Concentration
I
Certain manufacturing sites contribute 
significantly to our overall revenues. Disruptions 
at these critical manufacturing sites may have 
a material impact on order execution, which 
in turn may have an adverse impact on our 
financial performance.
We operate a network of 17 development 
and manufacturing facilities across India, the 
UK and North America. Strong order inflows 
and consistent execution across most sites 
help us mitigate and reduce concentration 
risk. For facilities with higher revenue 
contributions, we stay focused on minimising 
operational challenges and ensuring 
regulatory compliance, while simultaneously 
investing in capacity expansion and capability 
enhancement at other sites across our 
global network.

Data Protection and 
Cyber Security1
PPL’s advancement in technology and automation plays a critical role in 
enhancing operational efficiency, effectiveness and safety. By focusing on 
the integration of smart technologies, the Company aims to reduce manual 
intervention, an important requirement for managing increasingly complex 
and expanding workloads. The ‘CATALYST’ initiative is a key example of this 
approach, reinforcing PPL’s commitment to continuous improvement in IT 
operations and more streamlined business processes.
The Company’s key security protocols include multi-factor authentication, advanced encryption technologies and scheduled 
security audits. Together, these measures strengthen the integrity of PPL’s data protection framework.
The effectiveness and compliance of the Company’s internal 
IT infrastructure and information security management 
systems are evaluated through  periodic audits and self 
assessments. In addition, these systems were subjected 
to external audits during the last fiscal year, aligned with 
internationally recognised standards such as ISO 27001 and the 
NIST Framework.
To maintain high levels of accountability and transparency, 
compliance is continuously monitored through a combination 
of internal reviews and independent third-party assessments. 
Alongside established technical safeguards, including security 
controls and incident response protocols, PPL undertakes 
periodic evaluations to further strengthen its cybersecurity 
framework and overall resilience.
To foster a proactive security culture, the Company also 
conducts regular information security awareness programmes 
and training sessions. These initiatives are designed to enhance 
employee awareness, build internal vigilance, and strengthen 
defences against evolving cyber threats.

and protection.

third parties to maintain a secure ecosystem.

Oversight and Governance
PPL has well-established a governance framework to provide 
continuous oversight of information security and cybersecurity 
measures. The responsibility for advancing initiatives and 
driving their implementation rests with the Chief Information 
Security Officer (CISO) and the Chief Technology Officer (CTO), 
both of whom report to the Chief Information Officer.
Security-related updates are presented to the Board of Directors 
every six months. Quarterly review meetings are also conducted 
with the Chairperson to monitor progress, assess ongoing 
initiatives and address any security-related challenges. Quarterly 
reviews involve active participation from business heads 
to ensure alignment with security goals, with the Executive 
Committee receiving updates twice a year for ongoing oversight. 
This multi-layered governance structure facilitates prompt 
decision-making, strengthens risk management and ensures 
accountability across all functions. It is complemented by a 
formal escalation mechanism that allows internal stakeholders 
to quickly report incidents, vulnerabilities or suspicious 
activities through designated channels.
Ethical Information Technology  
and Privacy Policy

to safeguard personal and sensitive data while ensuring 
adherence to applicable data protection laws and regulations. 
This is supported by a structured IT Policy that governs the

maintains system integrity. In addition, the Company has 
established an IT Incident Management Policy designed to 
enable the timely detection, response and resolution of 
cybersecurity threats and data breaches.
Collectively, these policies form a sound and resilient 
framework for managing digital risks and ensuring responsible 
governance of technology.
Information Security Management Programmes
In line with its commitment to responsible business practices, 
PPL has established information security-focused business 
continuity plans to ensure the uninterrupted functioning of 
critical operations during potential cyber incidents or data-
related disruptions. To proactively manage risks, the Company 
conducts regular and structured vulnerability assessments, 
enabling the timely identification and remediation 
of security gaps.

Responsible Supply Chain
A transparent, responsive and 
responsible supply chain is central 
to PPL’s sustainability and risk 
management strategy. In a business 
environment shaped by global 
complexity, operational disruptions, 
cost volatility and evolving regulatory 
expectations, PPL is strengthening 
its supply chain framework to 
support efficiency, resilience and 
business continuity.
During FY2026, PPL focused on deepening the responsible 
supply chain practices established in the previous year. The 
emphasis was on stronger implementation, improved ESG 
risk visibility and enhanced supplier capability across the 
value chain. The Company’s supply chain spans multiple 
geographies and includes suppliers of raw materials, 
intermediates, services, logistics and other critical inputs. 
Given this scale and diversity, PPL follows a risk-based, 
collaborative and continuous improvement-led approach 
to responsible sourcing.
The Company’s Sustainable Procurement framework forms the 
foundation of this approach. It enables stronger engagement 
with suppliers across upstream and downstream operations, 
improves inventory visibility and reinforces ethical, regulatory 
and compliance standards across the wider value chain 
ecosystem. It also supports PPL in identifying and addressing 
environmental, social and governance risks while building  
long-term partnerships with suppliers.
PPL’s approach is aligned with globally recognised frameworks 
and initiatives, including the GRI Standards, ISO 20400, ISO 
26000, the United Nations Global Compact principles, EcoVadis 
and the Pharmaceutical Supply Chain Initiative (PSCI), of which 
PPL is a full-time member.
Supply Chain Framework
At the heart of PPL’s Sustainable Procurement framework are four pillars that align supply chain partners with the Company’s 
sustainability vision.
The first pillar is anchored in PPL’s Sustainable Procurement 
Policy, which sets the direction for responsible sourcing and 
reinforces the Company’s commitment to ethical, compliant 
and sustainable procurement practices. 
The second pillar focuses on supplier screening, with 
assessment processes designed to evaluate suppliers against 
environmental, social and governance criteria. This enables 
early identification of potential risks and opportunities in the 
supplier relationship.
The third pillar focuses on supplier capability building. 
Through structured training programmes, workshops 
and access to practical toolkits, PPL supports 
suppliers in understanding and aligning with evolving 
sustainability expectations. 
The fourth pillar moves beyond compliance to collaboration. 
It encourages deeper engagement with suppliers to foster 
innovation, strengthen circular practices and improve 
resource efficiency across the value chain.

The Supplier Code of Conduct (SCoC) serves as the 
foundation of PPL’s responsible supply chain framework and 
the overarching anchor for its four Sustainable Procurement 
pillars. It defines clear, non-negotiable expectations for all 
suppliers on ethical conduct, compliance and responsible 
business practices across the value chain.
The Code applies to PPL’s entire supplier base, including 
product suppliers, service providers and personnel suppliers. 
It sets out minimum expectations across human rights, labour 
practices, environmental responsibility, occupational health 
and safety and business ethics.
The SCoC is aligned with the four pillars of the EcoVadis 
assessment framework, covering Environment, Labour and 
Human Rights, Ethics and Sustainable Procurement. It is also 
aligned with the PSCI Principles, underpinning responsible 
practices specific to the pharmaceutical industry.
Supplier ESG Governance  
and Accountability
PPL has an embedded governance framework to oversee 
responsible supply chain management and ensure effective 
implementation of supplier ESG requirements. Executive 
management provides oversight of the Supply Chain 
Management programmes and drives accountability 
through structured monitoring, risk management, escalation 
protocols and periodic supplier performance reviews.
Governance responsibilities are integrated across existing 
organisational structures to enable clear ownership 
and cross-functional collaboration. The Procurement 
function embeds ESG considerations into supplier 
selection, contracting and performance management. 
The Sustainability team provides subject-matter expertise, 
supports ESG risk assessment methodologies and ensures 
alignment with global frameworks such as EcoVadis and PSCI. 
The Quality and Regulatory teams support supplier oversight 
through audits, compliance reviews and risk mitigation 
initiatives, particularly for critical suppliers. 
The governance framework also ensures compliance with 
the Supplier Code of Conduct and reinforces ESG clauses 
within supplier contracts. Periodic reviews of supply chain 
risks, supplier performance trends and ESG priorities are 
conducted at appropriate management levels. This helps 
embed sustainability into day-to-day procurement practices 
as well as strategic sourcing decisions.
Commitment to  
Sustainable Sourcing
PPL’s commitment to responsible sourcing is guided by its 
Sustainable Procurement Policy and reinforced through a 
structured Supplier Code of Conduct. Together, they define 
clear expectations for suppliers across human rights, labour 
practices, environmental responsibility and business ethics.
The Sustainable Procurement Policy sets the direction 
for responsible and inclusive procurement. It encourages 
preference for materials with lower environmental 
impact, supports supplier diversity and integrates ESG risk 
considerations into sourcing decisions. It also promotes 
innovative solutions that can help reduce Scope 3 emissions, 
enabling a more responsible, resilient and forward-looking  
supply chain.
Responsible Sourcing Safeguards
In line with PPL’s sustainability commitments, suppliers and 
partners directly sourcing palm oil are expected to procure 
Certified Sustainable Palm Oil / Roundtable on Sustainable 
Palm Oil and undertake third-party verification. This 
verification is aligned with no-deforestation, no-development 
on peat and no-exploitation principles from oil producers 
where palm oil is directly sourced.
PPL also promotes responsible mineral sourcing and requires 
suppliers to ensure that conflict minerals, including tantalum, 
tin, tungsten and gold, are not sourced from high-risk or 
conflict-affected areas within the supply chain.
In alignment with local animal welfare laws, PPL follows the 
3Rs principle of Replacement, Reduction and Refinement 
in animal testing. This approach prioritises scientifically 
valid alternatives wherever possible and seeks to minimise 
harm and distress.

Sustainable Procurement 
PPL’s Sustainable Procurement Management Programme 
is designed to create long-term environmental, social and 
economic value for stakeholders. The programme addresses 
key ESG risks across the supply chain, including climate 
change, water security, deforestation, labour practices, 
human rights and governance. It recognises the critical role 
of suppliers and partners in delivering sustainable outcomes.
The programme covers supplier screening, ESG risk 
assessment, training, guidance and compliance assessments, 
aligned with the Supplier Code of Conduct and international 
best practices. It is supported by coordinated oversight and 
procurement-level accountability, helping ensure that ESG 
expectations are embedded into sourcing decisions and 
supplier engagement.
PPL also reviews its purchasing practices to ensure 
consistency with ESG requirements and to proactively 
identify potential risks. Updated purchase and service order 
agreements include provisions requiring suppliers to provide 
assurance on adherence to ESG best practices, strengthening 
governance and accountability across procurement activities.
Supplier Screening and Assessments1
Screening Process
Supplier screening is an integral part of responsible supply 
chain management. The process integrates environmental, 
social and governance criteria along with overall business 
relevance into supplier selection and onboarding. The 
screening methodology considers country-specific, sector-
specific, commodity-specific, financial stability and regulatory 
compliance risks to ensure thorough due diligence.
This risk-informed and multidimensional approach helps 
build a responsible, resilient and value-aligned supplier base. 
ESG expectations are embedded from the outset through 
the Supplier Code of Conduct, which applies to all suppliers, 
including new suppliers. The code incorporates key social 
criteria aligned with international standards and reinforces 
expectations on responsible business conduct.
Assessments and Development
Supplier performance evaluation is a key component of 
supplier lifecycle management. Sustainability performance 
is assessed through the Supplier Sustainability Assessment 
Questionnaire (SAQ), developed in alignment with ISO 20400, 
ISO 26000, PSCI Principles, UN Global Compact, CDP, EcoVadis 
and S&P Global CSA.
Local Sourcing 
Local sourcing is an important part of this approach. PPL 
prioritises procurement from local suppliers, including Micro, 
Small and Medium Enterprises and women-led enterprises, 
to reduce environmental impact, strengthen supply chain 
resilience and support regional economic development. 
Procuring locally helps lower transportation-related 
emissions while fostering inclusive growth, innovation and 
stronger engagement with local supplier ecosystems. 
Local Sourcing for Responsible Growth
In FY2026, 25% of PPL’s total procurement spend was 
with MSME suppliers and 49% of total procurement 
spend was with India-based suppliers.
Alongside local sourcing, every supplier we onboard is subject 
to a structured screening process which will be discussed in 
the following section.
Based on assessments carried out during the reporting year, zero  concerns related to child labour, forced labour or 
compulsory labour were identified. These assessments support the ongoing strengthening of safeguards to address 
potential risks associated with child labour, hazardous work involving young workers and forced or compulsory labour 
across operations and the supply chain.
Supplier Assessment Focus Areas
Strategic suppliers are assessed through a self-
assessment questionnaire, which gathers essential 
information to support the long-term sustainable 
growth of both the Company and its supplier 
ecosystem. The questionnaire is being revised to 
strengthen disclosures across the following focus areas:

To assess supplier ESG maturity, the Company also

supplier performance and support informed engagement.
Assessments are primarily desk-based and are

independent third-party auditors or PPL team auditors

suppliers receive scorecards and feedback. Suppliers with

plans, technical guidance, resources and targeted training. 
Regular follow-ups are conducted to monitor progress and 
encourage continuous improvement.

Supplier Grievance Redressal and Escalation Mechanism
PPL recognises the importance of providing accessible 
mechanisms for raising concerns related to ethical conduct, 
human rights, labour practices, health and safety or other 
ESG‑related issues within the supply chain.
Suppliers, their employees and other relevant stakeholders 
may raise concerns through PPL's established grievance and 
whistleblower mechanisms, which are extended to cover 
supplier‑related issues. These mechanisms allow concerns 
to be reported confidentially and, where appropriate, 
anonymously, without fear of retaliation.
Reported concerns are reviewed and addressed in accordance 
with PPL's internal procedures, with appropriate escalation, 
investigation, and corrective actions undertaken as required. 
Where supplier‑related issues are identified, PPL engages 
with the relevant supplier to ensure timely remediation and 
alignment with PPL's ethical and sustainability expectations.
Training and Capacity Building – Suppliers
Following sustainability assessments, PPL adopts a structured 
supplier development approach to address identified 
gaps and strengthen ESG performance across the supply 
chain. Suppliers are supported through tailored technical 
guidance, resources and training interventions based on their 
assessment outcomes and ESG maturity levels.
Suppliers with identified gaps are provided with formal 
corrective action plans covering areas such as environmental 
management, labour and human rights, business ethics and 
governance systems. These plans are complemented by 
customised guidance and focused training to strengthen 
internal processes and controls.
Training programmes are designed to enhance awareness 
and implementation of sustainability practices. The 
modules cover PPL’s sustainability framework, requirements 
and expectations; Sustainability at Piramal; the pillars of 
sustainability; PPL’s SBTi commitment; ESG requirements; 
industry best practices aligned with PSCI Principles and 
EcoVadis pillars; climate action; decarbonisation awareness; 
responsible sourcing; and supplier expectations on 
ESG performance. 
Progress is monitored through regular follow-ups and 
periodic reviews to track implementation of corrective 
actions, assess improvement over time and ensure 
accountability. This approach enables suppliers to 
progressively align with PPL’s sustainability standards while 
fostering a culture of continuous improvement and long-term 
collaboration across the supply chain.
As part of Phase 1, virtual ESG training has been initiated for 
critical suppliers, covering 12% of the critical supplier base. 
An additional 66 suppliers have received targeted training 
sessions to deepen understanding and implementation of 
sustainability practices

To date, over 180+ participants from more than 120+ global

base. Building supplier capability is only half of the equation,

ability to manage these relationships which is discussed in 
the next section.
Internal Stakeholder  
Capacity Building
PPL recognises that a responsible and resilient supply chain 
is enabled not only by supplier performance, but also by the 
capability of internal procurement and supply chain teams. 
Accordingly, PPL is investing in structured capability-building 
initiatives to strengthen supply chain effectiveness while 
integrating sustainability considerations into day-to-day 
decision-making.
Internal training and development programmes are designed 
to enhance functional and cross-functional competencies 
across procurement and supply chain roles. These initiatives 
cover key supply chain management areas, including sourcing 
and procurement processes, supply planning and inventory 
management, supplier qualification and performance 
management, quality and regulatory requirements, logistics 
and risk mitigation, digital procurement tools and commercial 
and negotiation skills.
Alongside core SCM topics, relevant sustainability and ESG 
considerations are integrated into training programmes 
where applicable, enabling sourcing professionals to balance 
cost, quality, delivery, and sustainability parameters in 
procurement decisions. This supports responsible supplier 
selection, effective supplier engagement, and improved 
alignment with PPL’s sustainability objectives.
These initiatives are supported by broader capability-building 
programmes such as Catalyst Unleash, PPL’s Global SCM 
Capability Programme, which has strengthened functional 
expertise and essential skills across sourcing and supply 
chain teams. The year-long programme includes learning 
paths covering procurement, planning, logistics, supplier 
management, and sustainability integration.
Through continuous internal capability building, PPL aims 
to strengthen supply chain resilience, improve execution 
excellence and ensure that sustainability principles are 
consistently embedded across procurement and supply chain 
operations. This internal capability building feeds directly 
into NAVIGATE, our structured programme for de-risking 
relationships with critical suppliers.
De-risking Supply Chains – 
NAVIGATE Programme
PPL strengthened supply chain resilience through the 
NAVIGATE Programme, a structured supplier engagement 
forum developed in collaboration with the Quality and 
Regulatory Affairs teams. The programme focuses on 
identifying, assessing, developing and mitigating risks linked 
to critical suppliers, enabling stronger oversight and more 
reliable supply continuity. 
NAVIGATE supports enhanced quality oversight, regulatory 
compliance, business continuity, supplier performance 
improvement, knowledge sharing, OTIF performance, ESG 
and decarbonisation awareness. Through regular supplier 
engagement, site visits, vendor audits and performance 
improvement initiatives, the programme helps reduce supply 
disruptions, minimise product rejections, improve quality 
outcomes and strengthen long-term supplier partnerships.
The programme also enables faster response to supply 
chain disruptions by supporting strategic adjustments in 
inventory levels and modifications to production or sourcing 
schedules. In addition, PPL is diversifying its vendor base 
to reduce reliance on single-source suppliers for critical 
materials, further strengthening supply chain flexibility and 
operational continuity.

Supplier Assessment and Development
Supplier Collaboration: Scope 
3 Emission Reduction and 
Management
Value chain emissions represent a significant portion of 
PPL’s overall emissions profile. In the previous year, value 
chain emissions accounted for 85% of overall emissions, 
highlighting the need for focused supplier engagement. 
During FY2026, the Company advanced its five-year Scope 3 
supplier engagement roadmap, with a focus on strategic and 
high-spend suppliers.
The roadmap is centred on deeper supplier collaboration, 
emissions measurement, decarbonisation awareness 
and annual data collection. Key actions during the year 
included supplier capability-building sessions on emissions 
measurement, decarbonisation awareness sessions and 
systematic annual reporting. PPL initiated Life Cycle 
Assessments (LCA) for select consumer healthcare products 
to evaluate environmental impacts across the value chain, 
identify footprint reduction opportunities, and plans to 
expand LCA coverage to support product innovation, 
focusing on reducing lifecycle emissions and improving 
material efficiency.
These efforts are aligned with PPL’s broader sustainable 
procurement objectives and support improved transparency, 
preparedness and accountability across the supply chain. 
The Company also prioritises sustainable procurement, 
local suppliers and materials wherever possible as part of its 
approach to reducing Scope 3 emissions.
As a sustainable supply chain organisation, we recognise that meaningful climate action requires 
collaboration across the entire value chain. Building on our focus to map and reduce Scope 3 
emissions, we are deepening engagement with our supply partners to drive transparency, improve 
resource efficiency, and reduce the carbon footprint of our offerings. Together, we are embedding 
sustainability into the way we source, build, and deliver value for our customers and communities." 
Nihar Medh 
Senior Vice President and Global Head Supply Chain, PPS
