Product Excellence
Product Excellence, Technology and Automation and Operational 
Excellence are central to how we create long-term value at PPL. During 
the year, we upheld high-quality benchmarks, encouraged innovation 
and placed customer satisfaction at the core of our operations.
Product Quality and Safety1
Quality is a foundational pillar of PPL’s governance 
framework guiding how every product is developed, 
manufactured, released and monitored with discipline, 
scientific rigour and accountability as a critical driver 
of sustainable value creation. It underpins our ability 
to consistently deliver compliant, reliable products, 
maintain regulator confidence and safeguard long-term 
shareholder value.
At PPL, Product Quality and Safety is strengthened through 
strong quality standards, innovative methodologies and a 
focus on customer satisfaction. A well-defined, company-
wide quality model governs our global operations, anchored 
by a high-level quality policy which is supported by defined 
procedures, processes and review mechanisms. These 
systems are periodically assessed to ensure adaptability, 
continuous improvement and alignment with evolving 
regulatory expectations.
During FY2026, we advanced our transformation from a 
compliance-led model to a predictive, intelligence-driven 
quality ecosystem. Our priorities focused on strengthening 
global harmonisation, enhancing early risk detection 
and sustaining a state of perpetual inspection readiness 
across our network. We also strengthened product 
quality through enhanced governance and stronger 
regulatory preparedness. 
Embedded across the organisation, Quality operates as a 
disciplined and independent system that informs decision-
making, mitigates risk and reinforces stakeholder trust. 
In pursuit of empowerment and autonomy, the reporting 
structure of the entire Quality group culminates in the 
Chief Quality Officer (CQO), who directly reports to the 
Chairperson. This arrangement ensures the independence 
of the Quality group from operational and commercial 
verticals, enabling objective oversight, timely escalation and 
strong governance.
Strengthening Quality 
Foundations for Sustainable 
Excellence
In pursuit of proactively adapting to evolving regulatory 
expectations, PPL remain focussed on positioning 
quality as a sustainable strategic differentiator. 
During FY2026, the Quality Long Range Plan became 
fully operational, providing a forward-looking roadmap 
anchored on four pillars Process, Systems, People 
and Technology (including Automation). Aligned 
with our FY2030 strategic vision, The LRP reinforces 
our commitment to patient, customers, regulators 
and investors. 
Significant progress was made across all strategic 
priorities during the year, including strengthening the 
Quality Organisation Structure, accelerating digitisation 
initiatives, enhancing quality culture and maturity, 
driving process simplification, proactively responding 
to evolving regulatory requirements and advancing 
One PPL projects to foster greater alignment and 
harmonisation across the network.
Delivering product of good quality consistently, is central to our commitment to patients and 
partners. We continue to strengthen our quality systems, ensuring that excellence is built into 
every stage of the product lifecycle."
Rashida Najmi 
Chief Quality Officer
Driving Quality Excellence Through 
Governance, Oversight and Capability
 As we continue our transformation from a compliance-
focused model towards a predictive and intelligence-
driven quality ecosystem, our efforts remain focused 
on strengthening governance, enhancing oversight 
mechanisms and building organisational capability to 
sustain long-term quality excellence.
Our Quality Management Framework is founded on the 
principles of consistency, transparency and accountability. 
Critical quality systems across the network are aligned 
to centrally defined standards and governed through 
structured review forums, performance monitoring 
mechanisms and risk-based escalation processes. This 
enterprise-wide approach enables harmonised execution, 
strengthens decision-making and reinforces a culture of 
quality ownership across all sites and functions.
To further enhance oversight and risk visibility, we have 
established a multi-tier vigilance framework that supports 
proactive identification and management of emerging 
quality risks. Through robust governance forums, data-
driven reviews and early warning indicators, potential issues 
are identified and addressed before they impact product 
quality, compliance or business continuity. These mechanisms 
strengthen regulatory assurance while promoting a 
preventive and risk-based quality culture.
During FY2026, the Central Quality team continued to 
strengthen inspection readiness and quality oversight 
through a combination of scheduled corporate inspections 
and unannounced site assessments. This dual-layer approach 
provided independent assurance regarding the effectiveness 
of quality systems, enabled early risk identification and 
ensured consistent application of corporate quality 
standards across the network. Insights generated from 
these assessments were leveraged to drive continuous 
improvement and reinforce sustainable compliance practices.
People and Competence: Building a Future-
Ready Quality Organisation
Sustained quality performance is driven by empowered 
people and a strong culture. Our focus remains on developing 
agile, skilled and accountable teams capable of navigating 
an evolving regulatory and business landscape. Through 
continuous capability building, collaboration and shared 
ownership, we strengthen organisational resilience while 
advancing our commitment to patients, customers and 
long-term growth.
Our flagship capability-building initiative, Quality Academy 
2.0, was successfully completed during FY2026. The 
programme offers a structured, long-term certification 
journey covering critical and industry-relevant competencies. 
It helps strengthen technical expertise, build future-
ready capabilities and embed a consistent quality culture 
across locations.
Knowledge-sharing platforms such as Evolve and Quality 
Alerts supported the systematic dissemination of best 
practices and important quality updates across the 
organisation. Quench, our quality intelligence and regulatory 
tracking engine, continued to support timely monitoring of 
regulatory updates and consistent compliance across sites.
Together, these platforms support a stronger culture of 
learning, introspection and continuous improvement.

Digital Enablement for Scalable, Compliant 
Operations
Digital transformation remains central to PPL’s Quality 
strategy, helping strengthen data integrity, improve visibility 
and support scalable, compliant operations.
Under the CATALYST NexGen programme, implementation 
of SAP S/4HANA progressed during FY2026, strengthening

data integrity, laboratory efficiency and operational control.
The iLearn LMS was extended to 12 sites, supporting 
consistent training, competency management and capability 
building across the network. The eXForms platform

processes with structured digital records, improving visibility, 
traceability and data reliability.
During the year, AI-enabled assistive tools were introduced

quality control and patient safety by improving visibility 
across the supply chain. It enables faster identification of 
safety concerns, supports timely action, reduces the risk 
of counterfeit products and improves recall preparedness, 
wherever required. PPL complies with applicable regulatory 
requirements and is progressing towards the implementation 
of Quick Response (QR) codes or authentication codes on the 
primary packaging of relevant products.

between business priorities and operational outcomes.

===== PAGE 38 =====
The Daily Management System (DMS) forms the foundation 
of this shift. It helps teams detect issues early, solve them 
systematically and raise operating standards. By linking 
daily performance reviews with process standards and KPI 
objectives, it converts strategy into action at the shop floor. It 
also builds reliability, consistency and responsiveness across 
sites, keeping patient needs at the centre of execution.
Core Elements of DMS
The DMS framework is built around three priorities: detect, 
solve and improve.
Detect the problem:
The Performance Management System (PMS) uses visual 
control, standard and rapid response and tiered accountability 
processes to make performance gaps visible and ensure 
timely escalation.
Solve the problem:
Root Cause Problem Solving (RCPS) helps teams address the 
underlying cause of issues instead of treating symptoms, 
reducing recurrence and improving execution discipline.
Enhance the standard:
Capability at Gemba (CAG), Leader Standard Work (LSW), 
Standard Work Process (SWP) and Workplace Organisation 
(WO) strengthen capability at the point of work, reinforce 
standard practices and improve team ownership.
Together, these elements create a consistent operating 
rhythm that supports lean thinking, faster decision-making 
and sustained improvement.
Implementation Approach
DMS is being implemented through a structured, phased 
approach. Each DMS standard is sponsored by an SLT 
member, ensuring leadership ownership and direction. At 
the site level, the Operational Excellence (OE) team begins 
deployment through a DMS Boot Camp, followed by a site-
specific roadmap.
Sites identify their top SQCDP KPIs based on current 
operational priorities. KPI trees are then developed to 
understand performance drivers, identify root causes and 
focus improvement efforts where they matter most.
As deployment progresses, tier structures are established 
across the organisation. RCPS practices are initiated, 
leadership teams adopt LSW and CAG, and WO is piloted 
to reinforce discipline and standardisation. Leaders sustain 
progress through process confirmations, while DMS maturity 
assessments help evaluate progress, identify gaps and guide 
the next stage of improvement.
Maturity Level
Description
Level 1 - Beginning
DMS is piloted in 
select areas with active 
involvement from senior 
leaders.
Level 2 - Growing
Implementation expands to 
cover approximately 50% 
of the site, with increased 
engagement and training of 
leaders at SLT-1 and SLT-2 
levels.
Level 3 - Maturing
DMS is implemented 
across the entire site, with 
supervisors consistently 
practising DMS routines.
Level 4 - Guiding
DMS becomes fully 
embedded into site 
operations, delivering 
consistent results, strong 
ownership and long-term 
sustainability.
Capability Building
The DMS deployment is supported by a formal capability-building ecosystem anchored in Lean Six Sigma (LSS) programmes. 
Covering White, Yellow and Green Belt certification levels, these programmes combine classroom learning, project-based 
application and coaching. Implemented across all sites, they enable data-driven problem-solving and support measurable 
improvements in process performance.
The maturity of DMS implementation is defined 
across four levels:
Lean Six Sigma (LSS): Strengthening Structured  
Problem-Solving Capability
Objective
To build and institutionalise organisational capability 
in structured problem-solving through Lean Six Sigma, 
enabling data-driven decision-making, process optimisation 
and a sustained culture of continuous improvement 
across all sites.
Goal
To develop and drive high-impact Lean Six Sigma 
projects aligned with critical SQCDP priorities and deliver 
measurable business outcomes. The programme also 
aims to build organisational capability through structured 
coaching, governance and early leadership involvement.
Approach/Enablers
This capability-building journey is supported by 
three key enablers:
Guided Project Book
A standardised Lean Six Sigma project playbook covering 
DMAIC methodology, business case development, 
financial validation and review templates to ensure 
structured execution.
Leadership Alignment and Governance
Active involvement of HODs, Site Heads and mentors 
through structured reviews and accountability mechanisms 
to strengthen ownership and execution discipline.
Digital Integration through i2e Platform
End-to-end project tracking, review management and 
documentation through a unified digital workflow, 
improving transparency, consistency and governance.
Key Highlights 
Over 50 Yellow Belts have been successfully certified across 
India sites, strengthening the foundation of structured 
problem-solving. Green Belt training has been completed 
across SLT and SLT-1 levels, resulting in 86 projects 
launched across PQCDS, with 10 Green Belts certified so 
far. A structured roadmap is in place to scale up future 
certifications and sustain capability-building momentum 
across the organisation.

•	 R&D collaboration to refine solvent recovery 
methods and strengthen solvent usage 
protocols. 
•	 Process automation to reduce manual 
intervention and improve accuracy and 
efficiency in solvent management. 
•	 DMS-enabled structured problem-solving 
and shop floor performance tracking to 
support timely action.
•	 Digwal: Advanced solvent 
optimisation through critical 
solvent THF recovery via third party 
and improved solvent recovery 
plant efficiency. 
•	 Digwal and Ennore: Enhanced 
solvent dashboards to improve 
visibility, tracking and performance 
management. 
•	 Turbhe: Undertook crude loading 
optimisation and cleaning solvent 
reduction to improve solvent 
efficiency.
Yield Improvement
To systematically improve 
product yields across 
manufacturing sites, reducing 
Cost of Goods Sold (COGS) 
and strengthening EBITDA 
performance.
To progress towards True 
North benchmarks by 
reaching the 50th percentile 
by FY2026, 75th percentile 
by FY2027 and the 95th 
percentile by FY2028, 
while sustaining True North 
performance by FY2029.
•	 Real-time performance monitoring through 
the Qlik Sense-based Yield Dashboard to 
track yield trends, identify gaps early and 
drive timely improvements.
•	 Process optimisation in collaboration with 
R&D, TSD and cross-functional teams to 
refine process parameters.
•	 Structured Design of Experiments (DoE) 
to identify optimal process conditions and 
improve yield performance.
•	 DMS-enabled problem-solving, root cause 
analysis and daily performance tracking to 
sustain improvements at the shop floor level.
•	 Achieved US$1.6 Million in cost 
savings through targeted yield 
improvement initiatives.
•	 Key product-level improvements 
included Trazadone, Vibegron, 
Amiodarone and Paroxetine 
at Digwal; FCI 2579 at Ennore; 
Desmopressin, Leuprorelin,

Bempedoic at Pithampur.
•	 The Yield Dashboard was deployed 
at Digwal, Ennore, Morpeth 
and Riverview to strengthen 
performance visibility and tracking.
Turning Routines into Results
The DMS journey has gained strong momentum across the network. Boot camps have been completed at all PPS sites, along with PCC 
Digwal and Dahej. Aurora, Riverview, Sellersville, Ennore, Mahad, PDS and Digwal have achieved Level-1 maturity, while Pithampur became 
the first PPS site to achieve Level-2 maturity.
The impact is visible in reduced downtime, improved right-first-time performance and smoother shift handovers across sites, including 
Aurora, Sellersville, Riverview, PDS, Digwal and Pithampur.

across all operating parameters.

Core Focus Areas and Strategic Approach
PPL’s operational leadership approach begins with a detailed 
assessment of performance gaps against defined baselines 
or benchmarks. Based on this assessment, improvement 
opportunities are identified using OE tools. Structured 
action plans are then developed and implemented to close 
these gaps, strengthen execution and drive measurable 
improvements across priority areas.
Material and Cost Efficiency Initiatives
PPL drives focused initiatives that improve material efficiency 
and strengthen cost discipline across operations.
Material Savings
The Company is working to improve product yield and 
optimise the specific consumption of key raw materials. 
Efforts also include increasing solvent recovery and reuse, 
supporting both cost efficiency and responsible resource use.
Operational Expenses
PPL is also focused on improving productivity excellence 
through initiatives that optimise HR costs. In parallel, the 
Company advances energy conservation measures aimed 
at the efficient use of power, fuel and water resources.
Further details on energy conservation initiatives across 
sites are covered in the Responsible Operations section 
of this report.
Digitalisation for Enhanced Efficiency and 
Governance
Digitalisation has been central to sustaining efficiency 
gains over time. By using technology and Gen AI, PPL is 
automating governance processes, lowering dependence on 
manual intervention and enhancing accuracy and stability in 
operations. This digital shift is improving productivity while 
enabling more disciplined, transparent and efficient ways 
of working. Through value stream mapping, the Company 
identifies process inefficiencies, bottlenecks and delays 
and applies innovative technologies to eliminate waste and 
streamline workflows. These efforts are helping PPL build 
faster, more reliable and better-governed operations.
Asset Integrity
Driven by rapid technological progress that is making 
superior assets more attainable, PPL is focused on upgrading 
its infrastructure. This is helping improve efficiency, boost 
productivity and support consistent, long-term growth 
across operations.

Climate Change
Climate Governance
Climate-related risks and opportunities are managed through a structured governance framework that supports accountability 
and long-term resilience. To guide our transition towards a lower-carbon future, we have developed a comprehensive Carbon 
Reduction Plan (CRP), which outlines key actions, milestones and timelines to support achievement of Scope 1, Scope 2 and 
Scope 3 emission reduction targets. Climate considerations are integrated into business planning, enabling timely responses to 
emerging risks and supporting sustainable growth.
Climate Targets and Progress1
Target
	
↗Reduce Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions 
by 42% by FY2030 (with a baseline 
of FY2022), which aligns with the 
1.5°C decarbonisation pathway as 
recommended by the Science-Based 
Targets initiative (SBTi).
	
↗Reduce Scope 3 emissions with absolute 
contraction by 25% by FY2030 with a 
baseline of FY2022, which aligns with the 
2.0°C decarbonisation pathway.
Progress
The transition gained momentum through a combination of renewable 
power procurement, biomass-based energy, cleaner fuels and focused 
energy improvement projects. Together, these actions contributed 
to meaningful emission reductions and reinforced the resilience of 
our operations.

Energy Governance  
and Assessment
Energy stewardship is integrated into day-to-day operations 
through regular reviews and site-level engagement, ensuring 
that opportunities for improvement are identified and 
translated into action. Oversight is provided through the 
Sustainability and Risk Management Committee, which 
supports timely identification, assessment and management 
of risks. Regular assessments are conducted at all sites 
to find opportunities for reducing energy demand and 
improving utility performance. Based on these findings, we 
developed a roadmap to prioritise structured adoption and 
implementation of initiatives. Our governance structure has 
translated into tangible upgrades across our utility systems.
Utility Optimisation and 
Technology Upgrades
Comprehensive energy audits were conducted across all sites 
to identify opportunities for reducing energy consumption 
and improving utility efficiency. Based on the findings, a 
structured implementation roadmap was developed to 
prioritise projects according to impact and feasibility. Several 
initiatives have been successfully implemented, while others 
are being executed in phases.
We implemented more than 50 energy conservation 
measures, resulting in significant reductions in energy 
consumption and greenhouse gas emissions during the year.1

Key Highlights of FY2026
	
↗G-DAM (Digwal): Avoided 11,243 tCO₂e through 
renewable power procurement. 
	
↗Open Access arrangements: Sites including Ennore, 
Mahad, Turbhe, Pithampur, Dahej, PDS and PPDS sourced 
renewable electricity through group captive and third-
party open access mechanisms, using solar and wind-
solar hybrid projects. These initiatives contributed to an 
emissions reduction of 10,097 tCO₂e in FY2026.
	
↗Energy-from-Waste: EfW supplied 85% of the total energy 
demand of the Grangemouth facility.
	
↗On-site Solar: Installed rooftop solar capacity across 
Digwal, Dahej and Pithampur, generating renewable 
energy and avoiding 207 tCO₂e. 
Our Commitment to Continuous Improvement1
Energy stewardship is an ongoing journey. Through structured assessments, focused interventions and technology upgrades, 
we continue to strengthen our energy performance across operations. By embedding energy management into day-to-day 
operations, we are creating a strong foundation for responsible growth while supporting long-term sustainability goals.
Good practices and initiatives
Improving Performance of MEE through Heat Recovery and TVR Integration 
at Dahej
Challenge
The Multiple Effect Evaporator (MEE) plant consumed 
nearly 1,100 kg/hr of steam for effluent treatment 
operations. High steam demand increased energy 
consumption, operating costs and associated 
emissions, creating the need for a more effective steam 
management approach.
Our Approach
A two-phase programme was implemented to improve 
steam utilisation and recover waste heat.
Phase 1 – Heat Recovery
	
↗Installed an additional heat exchanger to recover heat 
from the Vacuum Thin Film Dryer (VTFD). 
	
↗Used recovered heat to preheat MEE feed streams.
	
↗Increased feed temperature and reduced steam demand 
during evaporation.
Phase 2 – Thermo Vapour Recompressor 
(TVR) Integration
	
↗Installed a Thermo Vapour Recompressor in the VTFD system. 
	
↗Reused process vapours to recover latent heat.
	
↗Reduced dependence on fresh steam by circulating 
available thermal energy within the process.
Impact
The initiative delivered measurable improvements in 
energy performance.
	
↗Approximately 55% reduction in steam consumption.
	
↗Significant reduction in energy costs.
	
↗Lower carbon emissions.
	
↗Improved process performance and resource utilisation.
Key Highlight
~55% 
Reduction in steam consumption through heat recovery 
and process integration

open access arrangements, Energy-from-Waste (EfW) solutions and on-site solar generation, we expanded clean energy 
sourcing across operations and reduced dependence on conventional power.

Good practices and initiatives
Transitioning Boiler Fuel from Fuel Oil to Propane at Sellersville
Challenge
The Sellersville facility operated three boilers using fuel oil 
for steam generation. Dependence on conventional fuel 
resulted in higher emissions, increased operating costs 
and greater complexity in fuel handling and maintenance. 
This created an opportunity to adopt a cleaner fuel while 
improving operational performance.
Our Approach
A fuel conversion programme was undertaken to 
transition all three boilers from fuel oil to propane, 
a lower-carbon alternative.
Key actions included
	
↗Conversion of all three boiler burners into propane-
based systems.
	
↗Installation of supporting infrastructure and propane 
storage facilities.
	
↗Integration of systems to ensure reliable and flexible 
boiler operations.
Impact
The transition delivered both environmental and 
operational benefits:
	
↗Approximately 10% reduction in greenhouse gas 
emissions compared to fuel oil.
	
↗Shift to a cleaner-burning fuel with lower 
carbon intensity.
	
↗Around 20% reduction in annual fuel costs.
	
↗Improved reliability and operational flexibility.
	
↗Reduced complexity in fuel handling 
and maintenance.
Key Highlight
Emission reduction of 589 
tCO₂e & ~20% reduction in 
annual fuel costs
 Sellersville, US
Recognition for Energy and Climate Performance
Piramal Pharma Limited, Digwal Site, was recognised as an 
“Excellent Energy Efficient Unit” at the 26th National Award 
for Excellence in Energy Management 2025, organised by the 
Confederation of Indian Industry (CII) at HICC, HYDERABAD. 
The site stood out for its impactful initiatives aimed at 
optimising energy use, reducing carbon footprint and 
fostering a culture of continuous improvement in energy 
efficiency. This national recognition reflects our commitment 
to sustainability, energy conservation and innovative energy 
management practices.
Air Emissions1
The Company monitors emission levels in line with 
applicable regulations, and these stay well within the 
prescribed limits. Air pollution control measures are built 
in from the design stage, supporting both compliance with 
statutory requirements and the Company’s commitment 
to sustainability and environmental responsibility in its 
pharmaceutical operations. These efforts contribute to 
maintaining regulatory standards while supporting long-term 
environmental goals.
Key Measures Implemented  
for Air Emission Control
	
↗Continuous Emission Monitoring Systems (CEMS): 
Implemented at API sites in India for emissions monitoring.
	
↗Gaseous Emissions: Controlled using new scrubbers with 
continuous pH monitoring as well as using bag filters.
	
↗Fugitive Emissions: Nitrogen blanketing on solvent storage 
tanks to prevent emissions.
	
↗Two-Stage Scrubbing Systems: Installed at major sites for 
regulatory compliance.
	
↗VOC Control: Double-stage condensers and activated 
carbon filters for effective Volatile Organic Compounds 
(VOC) management.
	
↗PM control at Coil Boiler: Equipped with Electrostatic 
Precipitators (ESPs) to filter particulate matter.
	
↗PM control at Briquette Boiler: Fitted with bag filters and 
cyclones to minimise particulate emissions.
We have focused on using safer, less toxic substances across 
our operations by applying green chemistry principles. This 
approach helps lower the risk of harmful emissions and 
supports our broader commitment to sustainability. Through 
these efforts, we continue building our environmental 
responsibility while promoting the well-being of people and 
the environment. Our responsibility extends beyond air 
quality to how we also manage noise across our sites which is 
described in the following section.
Noise Pollution
As part of our commitment to sustainability and 
environmental responsibility, the Company stays focused 
on managing noise pollution across its pharmaceutical 
operations. Controlling noise levels is essential to reduce 
environmental impact and support the well-being 
of our employees.
Key Noise Control Measures Implemented
	
↗Power-Generating Sets: Acoustic insulation has been 
installed to absorb and reduce noise generated by power-
generating equipment, helping to minimise noise pollution.
	
↗Equipment Noise Control: Enclosures and barriers have 
been fitted around noisy equipment such as compressors 
and pumps to contain and reduce noise emissions.
	
↗Noise Control Considerations: Work areas have been 
planned with noise control in mind, ensuring that 
operations are carried out in locations designed to mitigate 
noise impact.
	
↗Employee Safety: High-quality hearing protection devices, 
such as earplugs and earmuffs, are provided to employees 
working in areas with significant equipment noise.
	
↗Noise Impact Minimisation: Equipment has been 
strategically located in areas that minimise noise impact on 
work areas and surrounding environments.

Our Biodiversity Approach
Protect: Responsible Land Use  
and Biodiversity Conservation1
Most of the Company's manufacturing facilities are located 
within notified industrial estates, reducing the risk of 
significant impacts on biodiversity and sensitive ecosystems. 
As part of our approach towards responsible growth, the 
Company is committed to ensuring that future production 
sites are not established near protected areas, particularly 
those supporting vulnerable species and regions with 
high biodiversity value. Through careful site selection and 
responsible land use practices, we seek to minimise potential 
impacts on ecosystems and preserve biodiversity.

Cover across Operations

spaces within our facilities. In India, approximately 30% of the

improved ecological balance around operations.2

drives, guided by detailed surveys and soil characterisation 
studies to improve plantation success and increase 
green cover density.

sites as part of ongoing efforts to enhance green cover and 
promote environmental stewardship.

saplings across Indian operations, while continuing to 
evaluate additional opportunities for plantation activities on 
available land.

beyond Site Boundaries

Company sites at Dahej, PDS and PPDS. Opportunities 
to expand plantation activities on available vacant and 
leased land are being explored wherever feasible.
These initiatives support the restoration of green 
spaces and complement the Company's wider 
efforts towards climate action and responsible 
environmental management.

Water Stewardship  
and Waste Management
Water Stewardship1
Water security is integral to the continuity, resilience and 
responsibility of our operations at PPL. We are committed 
to being responsible towards our water usage and disposal 
by improving our water-use efficiency, reducing freshwater 
dependence, increasing reuse and recycling, ensuring 
responsible treatment and discharge across our sites.
Our water management approach is supported by structured 
interventions, continuous monitoring and targeted 
investments. During the year, the Company undertook a 
Comprehensive Water Use Assessment (CWA) across its sites 
in India, UK and North America. These assessments help us 
identify site-level opportunities to optimise consumption, 
improve reuse and strengthen long-term water resilience.
Across operations, we have implemented measures to 
minimise environmental impact by optimising water usage, 
treating and recycling wastewater and improving sustainable 
water use throughout our processes. We actively invest in 
and undertake CapEx and OpEx projects to systematically 
increase the quantity of reusable water and reduce 
overall water intake.
All our facilities are located within designated Industrial 
Development Corporation (IDC) areas and comply with 
government-mandated effluent discharge norms. Across our 
India sites, Sewage Treatment Plants (STPs) and Common 
Effluent Treatment Plants (CETPs) support responsible 
wastewater treatment and discharge with five sites equipped 
with Zero Liquid Discharge systems. Wastewater is treated 
and disposed of through authorised systems, ensuring 
adherence to applicable Central and State Pollution Control 
Board standards.

Comprehensive Water Use Assessment (CWA)2
The Comprehensive Water Use Assessment (CWA) is 
designed to uncover opportunities for reducing water 
consumption and to shape focused strategies that improve 
water management practices across sites. It facilitates 
a combination of near-term improvements and longer-
term investments by encompassing a broad spectrum of 
initiatives, ranging from micro projects and quick wins to 
larger capital expenditure (CapEx) programmes.
By implementing the CWA, we are improving visibility 
into site-level water usage, enabling the identification of 
optimisation opportunities, and directing efforts towards 
interventions that enhance water efficiency. The assessment 
establishes a systematic framework for responsible water 
stewardship, supporting sites in lowering freshwater 
dependence, increasing reuse and integrating sustainable 
water management practices throughout operations.
Micro Projects (Reduce, Reuse and Recycle)
Using the Reduce, Reuse and Recycle approach, we have 
identified and executed site-specific micro-projects that have 
delivered measurable water savings. These efforts focus on 
optimising consumption, recovering usable water streams 
and creating practical opportunities for reuse within day-
to-day operations.
At the same time, the Company is advancing a series of long-
term capital expenditure (CapEx) initiatives involving the 
adoption of new technologies and infrastructure upgrades 
to strengthen water reuse and recycling capabilities across 
its operations.
Reduce, Reuse and Recycle Implemented
	
↗Condensate recovery 
	
↗Auto backwash for multigrade filters 
	
↗Flow-regulating guns for equipment washing 
	
↗Water-less urinals 
	
↗Reuse of sanitised hot water 
	
↗Drip sprinkling systems for green belt development
Responsible management of water and waste is integral to our approach towards 
responsible operations. Our focus extends across day-to-day operations and 
long-term environmental priorities. Water stewardship is supported through 
efforts to manage consumption and increase opportunities for reuse, while 
our waste practices focus on reduction, segregation, recovery and responsible 
disposal. Continued attention to water conservation and waste management 
supports our efforts to strengthen environmental performance. By building these 
practices into operational processes, we are working towards greater resource 
stewardship and more sustainable ways of operating.

 Ennore, India
Site-level Initiatives Strengthening Water 
Stewardship
We implemented focused, site-level interventions to improve 
water efficiency, enhance reuse and support responsible 
wastewater management across operations. These initiatives 
reflect our commitment to reducing freshwater dependency, 
increasing treated water reuse and embedding circularity into 
daily operations.
	
↗At Ennore, four water-saving projects were undertaken, 
including the installation of pressurised pipes for drum 
cleaning. This improved cleaning efficiency and enabled more 
judicious water use, reducing consumption from 300 KL to 
200 KL.
	
↗At Ahmedabad PDS, a new RO plant was established to 
improve water quality efficiency and reduce wastewater 
rejection, saving 4 KL per day. The site also treats 70 KLD 
of water. Engineering controls supported further water 
optimisation and contributed to 10–15% compound savings 
at Ahmedabad PPDS.
	
↗100% water is recycled for afforestation and cooling purposes 
at Pithampur. A total of 37,169 KL has been recycled through 
ETP and STP, reinforcing the site’s focus on circular water 
use. ZLD and RO installation is also under planning, along 
with a groundwater recharge pit to support percolation of 
runoff water.
	
↗At Mahad, treated water is reused for gardening, reducing 
dependence on domestic water. The site has used 3,500 KL 
of water for gardening so far. An additional 100 KL storage 
capacity has been created for ETP water storage during 
periods when CETP operations are impacted by rising water 
levels, and a 30 KL STP plant has also been installed.
	
↗Overall water utilisation was studied and mapped in detail at 
our Turbhe site. Based on this assessment, the water piping 
system was modified to reduce wastage. These measures 
reduced daily water consumption from 125–130 KL to 90–100 
KL. The site also commissioned a water treatment plant to 
treat rejected water and reuse it in utilities, enabling savings 
of up to 20 KL per day. It operates an ETP capacity of 100 KLD 
and an STP capacity of 25 KLD.
	
↗At Rabale, daily COD analysis is carried out to monitor water 
quality and compliance. A dedicated water project has 
been undertaken, and the ETP plant has been refurbished 
to enhance both efficiency and capacity. Condensate water 
generated from TFA AHUs is collected in a dedicated tank and 
transferred to the rainwater harvesting system at the site lab. 
With a collection capacity of approximately 800 litres per day 
at 70–80% humidity, this amounts to nearly 2,00,000 litres 
annually, excluding dry winter conditions. The recovered 
condensate water is reused for gardening, reducing 
freshwater demand and supporting water conservation and 
resource optimisation.
	
↗Treated water is reused for cooling, with 60–70 KL treated 
every day at Dahej. The site has also installed a new ETP and 
a new MEE with a heat recovery system as part of its ZLD 
approach. This has reduced steam consumption by 300–350 
kg while lowering the amount of energy and water used in 
the process.
	
↗At the Digwal location, treated rainwater and sanitisation 
water were utilised as boiler feed, resulting in a reduction of 
freshwater consumption by 29,413 KL.
Collectively, these initiatives reflect our structured and multi-
dimensional approach to water management.
Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD) 
As part of its continued focus on water conservation, the 
Company has implemented Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD) 
systems across its API sites in India. These systems facilitate 
the recovery and reuse of treated water within utility 
processes, as well as for gardening and cooling applications, 
thereby significantly lowering wastewater discharge and 
reducing reliance on freshwater resources.
During the year, 1,91,887 KL of water was recycled and 
reused using ZLD system, leading to a corresponding 
reduction in freshwater intake. Building on ongoing 
improvements in engineering controls, the Company is 
targeting a further reduction in freshwater consumption. 
At present, ZLD systems are operational across Rabale, Dahej, 
Ennore, Pithampur and Digwal plants.
Rainwater Collection and Reuse
The Company collected 29,413 KL of rainwater during the 
year, which was treated and reused in utility operations 
wherever feasible. 
Rainwater harvesting also supports groundwater recharge 
and strengthens our water reuse commitment. By improving 
collection, treatment and reuse systems across sites, we 
continue advancing circular water use and reduce reliance 
on fresh water.
Good practices and initiatives
Raising the Bar on Wastewater Quality
What We Identified
During routine monitoring in August 2025, PPL identified 
that ammoniacal nitrogen levels in the plant’s wastewater 
effluent had reached 64 mg/L. The site team acted with 
urgency to understand the trend, trace the source and 
strengthen control over the treatment process.
How We Responded
The team began daily monitoring of ammoniacal 
nitrogen levels at the ETP inlet and outlet and analysed 
samples from each lab shaft to identify the source of 
variation. Multiple treatment trials were conducted using 
different dosing chemicals to arrive at the most effective 
reduction approach.
To improve treatment efficiency, air blowers were replaced 
to enhance aeration and maintain defined dissolved 
oxygen levels. Critical units, including the equalisation 
tank, treated water holding tank, secondary settling tank 
and oil and grease tank, were also thoroughly cleaned.
Good practices and initiatives
Smarter Cleaning, Lower Water Use at Ennore
Objective
At PPL, product quality and process integrity are central 
to responsible manufacturing. Equipment cleanliness 
plays a critical role in preventing cross-contamination and 
ensuring product purity. At the Ennore site, the objective 
was to improve drum cleaning efficiency while reducing 
water wastage, manual effort and safety risks.
Key Intervention
Earlier, drum cleaning was carried out using pipes with a 
1”–1.5” opening, leading to high and uncontrolled water 
flow. The process also required workers to manually shake 
the drums to remove residual material, which increased 
physical effort, extended cleaning time and resulted in 
avoidable water consumption.
To address this, the site introduced pressure guns 
designed for controlled and efficient cleaning. These guns 
provide better surface coverage, improved cleaning angles 
and stronger water pressure, enabling effective residue 
removal with reduced water use. The intervention also 
reduced manual handling, making the process safer and 
easier for workers.
Impact / Value Created
The initiative improved cleaning effectiveness 
while supporting responsible water use at 
the site. It helped reduce avoidable water 
wastage, improved process efficiency and made 
the cleaning activity safer for workers. The 
intervention reflects PPL’s focus on identifying 
practical on-ground improvements that create 
measurable environmental and operational value.
Impact Created
Through focused monitoring, root-cause analysis 
and strong site-level ownership, ammoniacal 
nitrogen levels were reduced from 64 mg/L to 6.4 
mg/L, well below the permitted limit of 10 mg/L. 
The site is currently maintaining levels at 4–5 mg/L.
This initiative reflects PPL’s commitment to 
proactive environmental management, disciplined 
execution and continuous improvement in 
wastewater quality.

Waste Management1
We generate both hazardous and non-hazardous waste 
across our operations. Effective waste management is 
critical to ensuring environmental protection, public health 
and safety and regulatory compliance. Our approach 
continues evolving with a clear focus on reducing landfill 
dependency, improving recovery across waste streams and 
building circularity.
PPL has established waste management guidelines across its 
operations, supported by site-specific Environment, Health 
and Safety (EHS) procedures. A structured programme is in 
place to identify, segregate, monitor and manage different 
categories of waste in line with applicable local regulations 
and internal standards. 
A detailed mapping of waste streams enables informed 
decision-making on treatment and disposal pathways. Based 
on material characteristics, waste is channelled towards co-
processing, pre-processing, recycling, incineration or other 
authorised disposal routes. This ensures that waste with 
energy recovery potential is effectively utilised, while low 
calorific fractions are managed through approved facilities. 
Non-hazardous waste is categorised and systematically 
directed towards authorised recyclers, strengthening 
material recovery and reducing landfill load. This practice 
supports the Company’s sustainability goals and helps 
minimise impact on the environment.

Good practices and initiatives
Advancing Responsible Waste Management Practices
Objective and Initiative
To strengthen waste management practices, improve 
regulatory compliance and reduce costs associated with 
waste handling non-conformances, the EHS team at 
Grangemouth undertook a targeted waste management 
improvement programme. The initiative included 
enhanced employee training, tighter operational controls 
and a comprehensive review of waste management 
procedures. In collaboration with Veolia, our waste service 
provider, waste management procedures were segregated 
into dedicated hazardous and non-hazardous waste 
streams, enabling the adoption of best practices and 
improved waste segregation across the site.
Waste and Effluent Management: Site-wise Initiatives1
PPL has undertaken multiple site-specific initiatives to improve waste management and effluent treatment practices across 
operations, with a focus on regulatory compliance, resource efficiency and reduction in environmental impact.
Impact
The initiative significantly improved waste 
management performance and compliance. 
Repacking charges resulting from waste-related 
non-conformances were reduced by 84%, from 
approximately £26,000 to £4,000. In addition to 
delivering substantial and sustainable cost savings, the 
program reduced compliance issues and enhanced 
overall environmental management at the site.
	
↗Ahmedabad PPDS reduced wastewater generation 
through engineering controls and process optimisation, 
improving overall resource efficiency.
	
↗Sludge management at Pithampur was enhanced 
through the installation of a paddle dryer with 100 KL/
hour capacity. The system reduced sludge generation 
by approximately 25% compared to the previous year 
and improved moisture reduction from 80% to 1-5%, 
lowering disposal volume and associated costs.
	
↗In Dahej, effluent management infrastructure was 
strengthened through the installation of a new ETP and 
the adoption of advanced treatment systems, including 
a Multiple Effect Evaporator (MEE) with heat recovery. 
These measures have reduced steam consumption by 
300-350 kg and supported the achievement of a zero-
landfill target.
	
↗Wastewater treatment infrastructure at Mahad was 
strengthened with the installation of a 30 KL STP 
and operation of an existing 140 KLD ETP, enhancing 
treatment capacity and operational resilience.
	
↗Turbhe adopted sludge drying practices to reduce 
moisture content prior to disposal, thereby minimising 
waste volume and improving handling efficiency.
	
↗Effluent treatment systems at Rabale, were upgraded 
through refurbishment of the ETP, including 
improvements to pumps, calibration and overall 
infrastructure. Additional measures included dedicated 
transportation for hazardous waste disposal, compliant 
handling of biomedical waste through authorised 
agencies, BMC, and development of underground 
storage to enhance STP capacity and retention time.
  Waste Segregation at Turbhe Site
Pre-processing for Co-processing
To improve the suitability of certain hazardous waste streams for co-processing, pre-processing techniques were applied 
to enhance their calorific value. This enabled better recovery of waste as an alternative resource and helped reduce landfill 
dependency through responsible and approved pathways.
Sludge management initiatives, including the paddle dryer at Pithampur and sludge drying practices at Turbhe, further support 
waste minimisation by reducing moisture content, lowering disposal volume and improving handling efficiency.
In FY2026, 25,569.03 MT of total waste managed was diverted from disposal through recycling, reuse, and other recovery 
operations, while 3,271.42 MT was directed to disposal through incineration, landfill, and other disposal operations.1

 Sludge Dryer, Pithampur

Good practices and initiatives
Mechanised Sludge Drying at Pithampur
Objective and Initiative
At PPL, responsible waste management is driven by 
the need to reduce waste volume, improve handling 
efficiency and minimise environmental and safety risks. 
At the Pithampur site, the objective was to improve the 
sludge drying process by reducing drying time, lowering 
disposal volume and creating safer working conditions for 
employees involved in waste handling.
Key Intervention
Earlier, sludge from the Effluent Treatment Plant was dried 
through a conventional process. This method required 
longer drying duration, large open areas, significant 
manual effort and higher use of energy and materials. It 
also increased disposal costs due to higher sludge volume 
and created potential health and odour-related concerns 
during handling, packaging and transportation.
To address these challenges, the site mechanised the 
process by installing a steam-based paddle dryer with a 
processing capacity of 100 KL/hour. The system enables 
rapid and uniform drying of sludge while reducing manual 
intervention and improving process control.
Impact
The intervention significantly improved waste 
handling efficiency at the site. Drying cycle time 
reduced by 99.3%, enabling faster processing 
and better operational reliability. Sludge 
moisture content reduced from nearly 80% to 
1–5%, resulting in a 50% reduction in sludge 
volume by weight.
The initiative has helped reduce disposal costs, 
improve energy efficiency and maintain safer, 
more hygienic working conditions. It reflects 
PPL’s focus on adopting practical technology-
led solutions that improve environmental 
performance while strengthening workplace 
safety and operational efficiency.
Environmental Emergency Response Measures
Disaster Management Preparedness
The Company has established a comprehensive Business 
Continuity Plan (BCP) to maintain critical operations during 
disruptive events such as natural disasters, technological 
failures or other significant incidents that may affect normal 
business functions.
The BCP covers a range of emergency scenarios across 
the organisation and sets out a structured approach for 
response, rescue and recovery. This strengthens operational 
resilience and supports timely restoration of critical activities.
As part of its business continuity efforts, the Company has 
installed automatic fire suppression systems supported by 
the Network of Volcanic and Atmospheric Change (NOVAC) 
system. This arrangement is designed to quickly contain and 
extinguish fires within designated areas, reinforcing overall 
safety preparedness and facility resilience. Preparedness 
for emergencies is complemented by equally disciplined 
management of the materials we use.
Material Management
PPL uses a diverse range of raw materials and substances 
across its operations. We are committed to responsible 
sourcing and to embedding sustainability considerations 
across the value chain. Our approach focuses on selecting 
materials, products and services that are environmentally 
and socially responsible while meeting the required 
standards of performance, safety and long-term value. 
We also work to reduce the use of hazardous materials 
by optimising processes, redesigning procedures and 
minimising the quantity of hazardous substances required 
wherever feasible.

Our People and Culture
Knowledge, Action, Care and Impact, our four core value pillars, shape 
our decisions across talent development, leadership practices and 
workplace culture. Care is reflected in the priority placed on fairness, 
respect and opportunity, guiding how we attract, develop and support 
our people across the organisation.
Human capital management is deeply integrated into the way we 
plan for growth and build long-term capability. A strong focus on skill 
development, leadership readiness and organisational resilience supports 
our ability to respond to evolving business needs while staying aligned 
with our purpose in healthcare.
With a permanent workforce of over 6,617 employees 
across India, North America, the United Kingdom and other 
key markets, we foster an environment that values diverse 
perspectives and experiences. Our people practices are 
anchored in a comprehensive ESG framework and guided 
by the Global Human Rights Statement, ensuring alignment 
with internationally recognised labour standards and a 
commitment to safe, inclusive and positive workplaces.
Capability building stays closely connected to business 
priorities. Structured policies, a consistent performance 
management framework and continuous learning 
opportunities support professional development and career 
progression. Through our Employee Value Proposition, 
Design Your Destiny, employees are encouraged to take 
responsibility for their growth, expand their capabilities and 
contribute meaningfully to organisational outcomes.
We strengthen leadership pipelines, reinforce ethical 
conduct and prioritise employee health, safety and  
well-being across operations.

Workforce Development and Progression
Hiring Framework and Practices1
Transparency and fairness underpin our talent acquisition 
philosophy. Every applicant is evaluated on clearly defined, 
role-specific criteria that focus on capability, experience 
and long-term potential. Decisions are made without 
consideration of age, gender, religion, race or any other 
personal attribute. This commitment to equal opportunity is 
not a guiding statement alone; it is embedded in each stage 
of recruitment to ensure consistency and impartiality for 
all candidates.
To support this, oversight mechanisms are built into the hiring 
framework. Recruitment processes and internal controls 
are periodically examined through independent third-party 
audits. Performance indicators such as sourcing efficiency, 
selection ratios, channel effectiveness and turnaround 
timelines are tracked via Qlik Sense dashboards. These 
insights are reviewed regularly by business leadership to 
maintain accountability and drive continuous improvement.
At the foundation of this system lies a future-orientated 
hiring strategy designed to attract talent aligned with 
organisational values and long-term sustainability 
goals. The entire recruitment lifecycle operates on a 
digital platform, with Oracle Recruiting Cloud enabling 
end-to-end management. Clearly articulated standard 
operating procedures govern every step, ensuring that 
the process stays structured, repeatable and resilient as 
workforce needs evolve.
Candidate evaluation combines technology with judgement. 
Online assessments, including psychometric tools and 
reasoning-based tests, form the initial layer of screening 
and are followed by formal interviews. Feedback from 
each interaction is formally recorded and reviewed, 
supporting objectivity and enabling informed comparisons 
across candidates.
Background verification is conducted through authorised 
external agencies and adheres strictly to applicable legal 
and regulatory requirements, including age verification at 
the time of appointment. Given the fully digital nature of 
recruitment, the Company does not retain original physical 
copies of candidate documentation, aligning operational 
efficiency with responsible data practices.

Employee Turnover
Retention, Engagement and Internal Mobility
Sustained organisational effectiveness is anchored in a 
closely linked focus on engagement, capability building and 
retention. These priorities are treated as interdependent 
rather than standalone initiatives, enabling the organisation 
to stay resilient while preparing for evolving business needs. 
Regular talent reviews form a critical part of this framework, 
allowing leadership to identify internal strengths and 
develop pipelines for roles that are both business-critical 
and future-ready.
Career growth within the organisation is supported through 
deliberate structures. The Career Opportunity Programme 
(COP) serves as an internal job marketplace, giving employees 
visibility into available roles and pathways across functions. 
By formalising internal mobility, the programme facilitates 
role transitions and skill enhancement while ensuring that 
existing talent is utilised more strategically.
This emphasis on building from within is reflected in 
progression outcomes. During FY2026, approximately 7.6% 
of employees were promoted following the year-end review 
cycle. In parallel, 17.4% of open roles were filled through 
internal movement, highlighting the organisation’s sustained 
focus on developing and deploying internal capability.
By aligning career development opportunities with long-term 
workforce planning we strengthen continuity while staying 
adaptable in a changing operating environment.
During FY2026, the Company reported a 23% turnover rate among its permanent workforce, with voluntary attrition accounting for 15%.1
Performance Evaluation and Goal Alignment2 
Performance management is treated as a disciplined, enterprise-wide process. A defined appraisal framework ensures that 
individual contributions stay tightly linked to broader organisational priorities. This structure spans the full performance cycle, 
from annual objective setting and mid-year performance conversations to formal year-end evaluations. For middle management 
and senior roles, the process is further strengthened through dedicated talent reviews that provide a well-rounded view of 
performance and capability.
Strategic alignment is built into the system at the outset. Goal setting follows a top-down approach, translating organisational 
priorities into measurable objectives across functions and levels. 
Evaluation outcomes are measured against clearly articulated metrics that balance business outcomes with sustainability 
considerations. By integrating these dimensions into performance assessment, the framework builds accountability while 
encouraging behaviours and results that support long-term value creation. This performance framework is anchored in a broader 
set of KPIs monitored consistently across the Company.

Employee Experience and Engagement
Employee engagement is shaped as an ongoing, organisation 
wide endeavour. The focus is on creating connection, 
recognition and a shared sense of belonging across roles, 
locations and teams. This approach blends virtual and on-
ground engagement formats, allowing relationships to form 
and strengthen regardless of geography. 
At PPL, growth, capability and engagement are closely 
interlinked. Well defined talent management processes 
support long-term development, including periodic talent 
reviews, succession planning and individual development 
plans. Regular check-ins in Connect conversations provide 
a structured forum for dialogue between managers 
and employees, covering performance, aspirations and 
development needs. Through these mechanisms, every 
employee receives formal feedback on both performance 
and career progression.

Rewards and Recognition
Recognition plays an important role in encouraging 
behaviours that reflect our values. Our rewards and 
recognition framework is designed to celebrate 
excellence, collaboration and meaningful contribution 
while creating an environment where employees feel 
acknowledged and valued.
As an equal opportunity employer, we ensure that all 
employment-related decisions, including recognition, are 
based on merit. The rewards and recognition framework is 
structured to acknowledge a broad range of contributions, 
from consistent everyday effort to initiatives that drive 
significant business outcomes. Programmes are adapted to 
local cultural and operational contexts across geographies to 
ensure relevance and equitable participation at all levels.
Recognition Mechanisms
Recognition and shared experiences play a central role 
in building connection and is delivered through multiple 
channels to encourage timely and meaningful appreciation:
	
↗Gratitude Notes and Thank You Cards recognise employees 
who demonstrate commitment and support beyond 
routine responsibilities.
	
↗On-the-Spot Awards provide immediate recognition 
for individuals who display initiative or deliver 
exceptional outcomes.
	
↗Excellence Awards form a formal programme that recognises 
individuals and teams for value creation through efficiency, 
performance and sustainable practices. Employees may 
self-nominate, and awardees are selected through leadership 
evaluation at each site.
Employee Benefits1
We provide a comprehensive benefits framework designed 
to support employee safety, well-being and long-term 
financial security across geographies. The Company’s 
approach extends beyond statutory requirements, reflecting 
a sustained focus on employee care throughout the 
employment lifecycle.
In India, enhanced benefits include future service gratuity, 
term insurance, flexible benefit pay and bereavement leaves, 
supporting both financial security and family-related needs. 
Globally, core benefits such as group medical insurance, 
life and disability coverage, and retirement provisions are 
extended to all eligible employees. 100% of employees 
earning above the minimum wage receive full benefit 
coverage. These benefits are not extended to third-party 
contractual staff who are not on PPL’s payroll.
We ensure adherence to minimum wage laws and statutory 
mandates across all locations, supported by periodic audits. 
Employees are typically provided a minimum notice period 
of 12 weeks2, or a notice period aligned with employment 
agreements, prior to the implementation of significant 
operational changes that may substantially affect them. 
Temporary employees receive all benefits mandated 
under local regulations, excluding select programmes 
such as employee stock options and performance-linked 
pay revisions. 
Employee well-being is supported through targeted 
programmes and daily welfare facilities. The Piramal 
Rx programme, active across India and North America, 
provides a structured mental health and wellness framework 
supported by a year-round calendar of activities. Additional 
support is offered through subsidised transportation and 
canteen facilities, while work-life balance is promoted 
through a flexi-work policy and employee engagement 
initiatives. Site-level engagement calendars ensure that long-
service milestones and local celebrations stay aligned with 
global recognition standards.
Retirement Plans and Long‑Term Security3
To strengthen long‑term financial stability, we offer 
structured retirement benefits through Provident Fund, 
Gratuity and Superannuation schemes. During FY 2025–26, 
Company’s contributions amounted to ₹108.28 crore towards 
the Provident Fund, ₹8.08 crore towards Gratuity, and ₹0.32 
crore towards the Superannuation Fund.
Every decision we make today carries the opportunity to create a more resilient business and 
a more sustainable future. By embedding sustainable practices across our operations, we are 
raising the standard for what responsible manufacturing can achieve.”

Family Care Benefits and Support
Parental support leave is extended to all parents, irrespective of gender, under a dedicated parental support framework. For 
FY2026, the return‑to‑work rate following parental leave was 99.6%, and the retention rate stood at 79%.
We support working parents through childcare assistance for 
employees with children below six years of age. Support is 
provided through in house crèche facilities and partnerships 
with external childcare providers, enabling employees to 
manage professional and personal responsibilities effectively.
Flexible work arrangements are available based on role and 
operational requirements. These include remote working 
options, flexible working hours, shift-based schedules and 
compressed workweeks. Established systems and guidelines 
ensure consistent implementation while maintaining 
operational continuity.
Employee Health and Wellness Programmes
During FY 2025-26, the Piramal Rx Employee Wellness 
Programme was further strengthened, reinforcing the 
Company’s focus on building a healthier and more supportive 
workplace. The programme is organised around three pillars: 
physical wellness, mental wellness and financial wellness.
Over 108 wellness initiatives were conducted during the year, 
with focused interventions on financial literacy, preventive 
healthcare and mental resilience. These initiatives reflect 
the Company’s continued emphasis on care as a core 
organisational value.
Physical Wellness 
Our Physical Wellness initiatives are aimed at promoting 
active lifestyles, preventive care and community engagement. 
Employees are encouraged to participate in global fitness 
initiatives such as International Yoga Day along with activities 
including Zumba sessions, sports tournaments and large-
scale events such as Office Olympics and Winter Olympics. 
Preventative health sessions cover key topics such as 
diabetes, hypertension, stroke prevention, lifestyle diseases 
and seasonal health risks. These efforts further complement 
our blood donation drives and community health initiatives 
across sites, sharpening our focus on proactive healthcare 
and well-being. Investments in employee well-being 
include the inauguration of facilities such as a new crèche 
and recreational amenities, alongside initiatives like step 
challenges, sports tournaments and wellness competitions to 
drive sustained participation. 
Mental Wellness 
Mental well-being is a key pillar of our employee value 
proposition supported by the Employee Well-being & 
Assistance Programme (EWAP), offering counselling and 
support services for employees and their families. Global 
awareness initiatives, including World Mental Health Day, 
focus on emotional resilience, while structured interventions 
across sites addressed stress management, mindfulness and 
workplace well-being. Wellness Wednesday sessions, mental 
health risk assessments, and awareness campaigns further 
reinforce a proactive and supportive approach. 
Financial Wellness 
Financial wellness initiatives are focused on building financial 
awareness and enabling informed long-term financial 
planning. Employees are supported through sessions on 
personal finance, tax planning, pension awareness and 
investment fundamentals. Select locations also offer on-site 
financial advisory support, complemented by dedicated 
learning modules under Wellness Wednesday sessions, 
enhancing financial literacy.
This year marked a significant expansion in both scale 
and impact of our wellness initiatives, with a strong focus 
on preventive care, sustained engagement and rounded 
employee well-being.
*Includes employees returning from parental leave carried over from the previous reporting year.
Building Capabilities and Leadership 
Skills through Learning and Development 
Programmes1 
Investing in employee well-being goes hand in hand with 
investing in their professional growth. Capability building 
is approached as a long-term investment in leadership 
depth, functional excellence and organisational readiness. 
Structured learning interventions across levels strengthen 
decision-making, collaboration and execution effectiveness in 
an increasingly complex business environment.
Leadership Academy
The Leadership Academy anchors the Company’s leadership 
development efforts, offering tailored interventions aligned 
to organisational priorities and role readiness.
Piramal Executive Council (PEC)
The leadership team, comprising 16 senior leaders, 
participated in a two-day immersive workshop designed 
to set strategic direction for the new financial year. The 
programme focused on building leadership mindsets and 
capabilities required to operate in a BANI environment. 
Sessions emphasised resilience, agility, clarity in 
ambiguity and effective decision-making amid evolving 
external challenges.
ScaleUp Programme
The ScaleUp initiative focused on strengthening leadership 
alignment with the organisation’s strategic direction while 
enhancing the ability to energise, influence and lead teams 
across functions. The programme reached over 410 leaders 
globally and integrated Hogan assessments, supported 
by individual debriefs facilitated by external coaches. 
Leaders actively co facilitated learning sessions, fostering 
shared ownership and a common leadership language. The 
concepts were applied to advance the long-range plan across 
businesses and functions.
Piramal Leadership Series (PLS):
The Piramal Leadership Series is the flagship programme 
under the Leadership Academy. Designed as a band wise 
learning framework, PLS addresses role-specific leadership 
readiness across Bands 1, 2 and 3. Each journey is a 45-day 
blended learning experience aligned with the Piramal Success 
Factors and supports both current role effectiveness and 
future role transitions.
During FY 2025-26, five PLS journeys were completed:
	
↗Executive Excellence and Leading Self for Band 1
	
↗People Excellence and Business Excellence for Band 2
	
↗People Excellence for Band 3
The total programme coverage was 250 participants. In 
recognition of the programme’s impact, the Company 
received the Gold Award at the Economic Times Human 
Capital Awards 2026 in the category of Excellence in 
Leadership Development Programme.
Functional Academies
To deepen functional expertise and enable sustainable 
performance, we operate multiple functional academies 
across key domains. These include HR, Finance, Shared 
Services (Gurukul), Quality, Supply Chain Management 
(Catalyst Unleash) and Global Engineering and 
Maintenance (iLead).
In FY 2025-26, 514 employees participated in learning 
programmes delivered through the Functional Academies, 
supporting both individual growth and organisational 
capability building. This functional expertise is reinforced 
by newer programmes designed to build cross-
organisational collaboration.
Building a Culture of Learning
To improve collaboration and execution within complex 
organisational structures, two new leadership capability 
programmes were launched during FY 2025-26:
	
↗Cross culture Compass engaged 42 employees, focusing 
on building cross cultural awareness and equipping 
participants with practical tools to collaborate effectively 
across geographies.
	
↗Matrix Momentum reached 23 employees and 15 managers, 
supporting teams working in matrix environments by 
strengthening role clarity and managing interdependencies 
aligned with business priorities.

The platform provides access to over 10,000 courses and

Induction and Integration
A structured induction framework supported the smooth 
integration of 1,050 new joiners through Day 1 induction

Inculcating Sustainability through Focused 
Training
We reinforce sustainability awareness through structured 
learning initiatives that build shared understanding across the 
organisation. An in-house e-learning module on sustainability 
was developed to provide employees with a clear view of 
sustainability fundamentals, its relevance to the business, 
the value of integration and our sustainability framework 
and strategy. The module achieved 100 percent completion 
among active employees, reflecting strong engagement 
and affirming the Company’s commitment to embedding 
sustainability into everyday thinking and practice.
Security and contract worker trainings1
We place a strong emphasis on equipping contract workers 
and security personnel with the knowledge required to 
operate responsibly and safely. All contract workers receive 
structured training on sustainability principles, human 
rights and occupational health and safety, aligned with the 
Company’s global human rights statement and ESG policies. 
Clear procedures, ongoing guidance and regular monitoring 
help reinforce adherence and assess the effectiveness of 
these programmes over time.
Security personnel undergo dedicated and comprehensive 
training aligned with our Human Rights Statement and 
sustainability principles. These sessions cover human rights 
awareness, ethical conduct and strict compliance with 
security protocols, ensuring that all security staff are well 
prepared, informed and fully compliant with organisational 
and regulatory expectations.
Mandatory Learning and Compliance
A strong focus on compliance ensured timely completion 
of mandatory learning modules for all employee groups. 
Simplified structures and video-based formats were adopted 
to improve learner engagement. During FY 2025-26, 100% 
completion of mandatory learning modules was achieved 
across permanent and contractual employees. Alongside 
compliance and capability, we place equal emphasis on 
building an inclusive workplace culture.

Building a Culture of  
Inclusion and Belonging
At Piramal Pharma Limited, inclusion and belonging are 
foundational to building a respectful, engaged and high-
performing organisation. We are committed to creating a 
workplace where every individual feels valued, supported 
and empowered to contribute fully. During FY 2025-26, we 
strengthened the OnePPL culture through initiatives that 
foster connection, representation and shared understanding 
across geographies.
Fostering Inclusion through  
Awareness and Celebration
Cultural and social observances play a meaningful role in 
deepening inclusion and allyship across the organisation. 
Throughout the year, employees across India, the United 
Kingdom and North America participated in celebrations 
and awareness initiatives that reflected both global and 
local contexts.
During Pride Month, employees were engaged through 
awareness campaigns, dialogue sessions and community-led 
initiatives aimed at promoting understanding and allyship. 
International Men’s Day was marked across sites through 
appreciation-based initiatives recognising the contributions 
of male colleagues, alongside sensitisation efforts focused on 
overall well-being, including prostate cancer awareness.
Additional observances included the International Day 
of Persons with Disabilities, which featured sensitisation 
sessions to enhance awareness of inclusion for differently 
abled individuals in the workplace. International Women’s 
Day celebrations focused on physical well-being and 
hormonal health, complemented by storytelling sessions 
hosted by the Women Employee Resource Group where 
employees shared personal journeys and experiences. Site-
level celebrations included wellness initiatives, interactive 
engagements and peer appreciation through testimonials and 
thank-you cards.
Awareness and behavioural change were further 
reinforced through DIBA sensitisation and unconscious 
bias workshops. These sessions engaged leaders across 
levels, prompting reflection and strengthening personal and 
professional commitment to recognising and mitigating bias 
in the workplace.
National and global observances such as Independence Day, 
Republic Day and Veterans Day were also commemorated, 
reinforcing collective identity, respect and shared purpose.
Company wide events such as Family Day, annual gatherings 
and milestone celebrations provide common touchpoints, 
while local initiatives reflected cultural and regional 
diversity. We also celebrated the third edition of Family 
Day, “Piramal Kutumbakam 2025”, across our India sites, 
bringing employees and their families together for a day of 
joy, connection and shared experiences. The celebrations 
featured interactive games rooted in the organisation’s 
values and other thoughtfully curated fun activities around 
the theme of “World Tour of the Seven Wonders”, offering 
children an engaging and educational glimpse into global 
cultures. Festival observances, sports events and site-specific 
activities created opportunities for informal interaction, 
reinforcing camaraderie across teams.

Employee Resource Groups  
and Belonging Initiatives
Employee Resource Groups play a central role in advancing 
inclusion by creating platforms for dialogue, learning 
and representation. During the year, the Women, Pride 
and Veterans ERGs actively engaged employees across 
regions through structured programmes and community-
building initiatives.
The Women ERG hosted Trailblazers sessions across Piramal 
Consumer Healthcare and PPL Kurla, creating spaces for 
storytelling, networking and reflection. The Pride ERG at 
Riverview led monthly discussions focused on inclusive 
language, pronoun awareness and visual storytelling 
Labour Practices and Human Rights
PPL upholds internationally recognised labour standards and 
human rights principles across its operations and value chain. 
These commitments are guided by the Global Human Rights 
Policy Statement, aligned with the United Nations Universal 
Declaration of Human Rights and the UN Guiding Principles 
on Business and Human Rights.
The Company maintains a zero-tolerance approach towards 
harassment, discrimination, child labour, forced labour and 
human trafficking. Any form of harassment, whether sexual 
or non-sexual, and discrimination on the basis of gender, age, 
ethnicity, religion, disability or other protected characteristics 
is strictly prohibited. Safeguarding a safe, respectful and 
secure work environment is a priority across all locations. 
Working Conditions, Compensation  
and Well being
The Company ensures that wages meet or exceed 
applicable living wage benchmarks and that employees are 
compensated fairly for additional or atypical working hours. 
Living wage standards are periodically reviewed by the 
Remuneration Committee to support pay equity. Monitoring 
of working hours, annual leave utilisation and overtime 
practices contributes to employee well-being.
The Company offers family-supportive policies, including 
parental leave, flexible working arrangements, remote work 
options and childcare support for employees with children 
below six years of age. Comprehensive healthcare coverage, 
transparent salary structures, bonus schemes and housing-
related benefits further support overall employee welfare.

launched Veterans ERG engaged its global cohort 
through focused sessions that strengthened cross 
site connection and understanding. Collectively, 
these forums contributed to building a culture where 
diverse voices are recognised and belonging is actively 
nurtured. Our commitment to inclusion is underpinned 
by a broader framework of labour and human 
rights protections.

Freedom of Association and  
Collective Bargaining1  
We respect the right of employees to freedom of association 
and collective bargaining. Employees may freely form, join 
or refrain from joining trade unions or representative bodies 
without fear of retaliation, harassment or intimidation.
In India, 96% of permanent workers are members of worker 
associations or unions and were covered by formally 
elected employee representatives and collective bargaining 
agreements. Structured two way communication mechanisms 
enable open dialogue between employees, representatives 
and management. Engagement with work councils and 
employee representatives supports transparency and shared 
accountability.
During the year, we ensured compliance with local labour 
laws, strengthened grievance mechanisms and engaged 
constructively with worker representatives. Select suppliers 
were evaluated under the Supplier Sustainability Framework, 
with assessments indicating no identified risks related to 
human rights, health and safety or working conditions.
Child and Forced Labour Prevention2
To prevent child and forced labour, PPL enforces strict age 
verification processes and conducts periodic internal and 
external audits, including social impact assessments for 
contract workers. Employees receive awareness training 
to recognise, prevent and report potential risks related to 
exploitation. While employment of individuals below the age 
of 18 is prohibited, safeguards are applied in jurisdictions 
where young workers may be legally employed.
Social Dialogue, Gender Equality and  
Capability Building
The Company works towards reducing the gender pay gap 
and ensuring equitable remuneration for men and women. 
Regular social dialogue with employee representatives 
supports transparency and trust. Reskilling and upskilling 
programmes, particularly in the context of industrial and 
climate-related transitions, are implemented to strengthen 
adaptability and workforce readiness.
Targeted sensitisation initiatives address unconscious bias 
and inclusive behaviours. In India, gender-agnostic POSH 
training is conducted for employees, with ongoing capability 
development programmes for internal committee members. 
These efforts reinforce a respectful, safe and gender-neutral 
workplace. These commitments are reinforced by the 
accountability mechanisms in place within PPL described in 
the following section.
Project Vardhana
Project Vardhana serves as a catalyst for community 
development around PPL’s Digwal plant, with a clear 
focus on building sustainable, women-led impact. 
Now in its fourth batch, the initiative has trained 
approximately 140 women in STEM-related skills, 
opening pathways to employment within Piramal 
Pharma as well as across other organisations. By 
developing technical capability and employability, 
Project Vardhana supports women’s economic 
participation while contributing to long-term, 
systemic change across local communities and the 
broader manufacturing ecosystem.
